### 2026-10-25 serialize worker report-version allocation by case

- **Finding:** API report-draft creation locks and refreshes the case row before reading the latest case/report-type version. The report stage in `run_variant_analysis` read the maximum version without that lock. Concurrent analyses for one case could select the same next version; the uniqueness constraint would force one execution into retry and could complicate artifact reconciliation.
- **Correction:** the worker now locks and refreshes the owning case before checking for an existing draft or allocating a new report version. Missing case lineage fails explicitly. This matches the API's existing lock protocol and the database uniqueness scope.
- **Regression evidence:** the production worker report-stage failure/retry test now tracks row-lock requests and asserts the case lock is requested on the failed attempt and again on retry. PostgreSQL has a dedicated concurrent case-wide version allocation integration test; the latest CI run on prior commit passed, and this code change will be exercised by the next exact-SHA CI run.
- **Verification:** report-stage recovery, sign-out, report contract/finalization, artifact compensation/replay, reviewer-to-signed-report and reportability tests — **66 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Python compilation and `git diff --check` passed. This Linux run uses SQLite and does not itself demonstrate PostgreSQL lock behavior or simultaneous worker contention.
- **Status:** local implementation/test is **VERIFIED** for lock request and retry flow. PostgreSQL contention evidence is pending exact-SHA CI after push. Phase A remains **PARTIAL**; live/deployed sign-out, storage recovery and laboratory qualification are not established.

### 2026-10-25 narrow HPO resource initialization failure handling

- **Finding:** HPO ontology and HPOA initialization wrapped resource-plan resolution, execution-contract validation, provider registration and pinned-file parsing in `except Exception`. An unexpected internal/database/programming error could therefore be converted to an optional-resource limitation and the evidence stage could continue as if only the resource were unavailable.
- **Correction:** these boundaries now catch only the expected profile/runtime resource, execution-contract, provider-registry, and respective HPO/HPOA validation exceptions. Unexpected exceptions reach the existing evidence-stage failure/retry path instead of being reported as missing scientific evidence.
- **Regression evidence:** expanded the actual worker integration test with injected unexpected resolver errors for both `PHENOTYPE_ONTOLOGY` and `DISEASE_PHENOTYPE_ASSOCIATION`; each asserts a failed evidence stage (`EVIDENCE_BUILD_FAILED`) without provider-limitation metadata. Existing invalid-ontology behavior remains covered.
- **Verification:** targeted HPO failure cases and invalid-resource fixture — **3 passed**. Related worker/recovery, resource, ClinGen, evidence, HPO OBO/HPOA provider and reanalysis suites — **84 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. The unfiltered backend command `PYTHONPATH=. DATABASE_URL=sqlite:////tmp/siraloom-full-backend-including-pdf.db REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0 GENEBE_ENABLED=false GNOMAD_ENABLED=false .venv/bin/python -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp=/tmp/siraloom-full-backend-including-pdf -q tests --tb=short` — **1,348 passed, 46 skipped**, one same warning. The two PDF renderer tests passed in this Linux environment. `.venv/bin/python -m py_compile` and `git diff --check` passed. No live HPO resources or lab deployment were used.
- **Repository state:** code and ledger evidence are pushed to `main` in `2063883`; exact-SHA CI run [40185706536](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/40185706536) passed. Unrelated untracked `ci.db` and `siraloom.egg-info/` are preserved. Phase A remains **PARTIAL**; this does not establish live authentication, deployed tenant isolation, truth-set validation, or deployment qualification.

### 2026-10-25 distinguish ClinGen provider failures from evidence-builder failures

- **Finding:** three evidence-provider blocks caught every `Exception` around both the external/provider query and subsequent local persistence/evidence mapping. As a result, a programming error or a database failure after a successful provider response could be recorded as a provider limitation, obscuring the true stage failure and misrepresenting evidence availability.
- **Correction:** the ClinGen Variant Pathogenicity, ClinGen Gene-Disease Validity, and ClinVar blocks now catch only their adapters' declared provider error types. Unexpected builder, persistence, and programming exceptions propagate to the workflow's normal stage-failure handling.
- **Regression evidence:** expanded the actual worker/replay integration test with injected internal evidence-mapping failure and typed ClinGen provider failure. The tests assert the internal failure produces `EVIDENCE_BUILD_FAILED` without provider-limitation metadata while preserving the successful provider execution record; a typed provider failure is recorded as `CLINGEN_VARIANT_PATHOGENICITY_QUERY_FAILED` and leaves the evidence checkpoint recoverable for replay.
- **Verification:** focused injected-failure cases — **2 passed**. Related worker, resource-execution, ClinGen-provenance, evidence-engine, reanalysis-provenance and recovery suites — **48 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx TestClient deprecation warning. Python compilation using `.venv/bin/python` and `git diff --check` passed. Ruff `--select F` on the legacy workflow reports seven pre-existing unused imports/variables outside this correction; they were left untouched. No live external provider or deployed lab was used.
- **Repository state:** changes are currently uncommitted on `main` at `ae1577070daa7be7b468bb050f1933603c85285d`; `origin/main` resolves to the same SHA. Preserve unrelated untracked `ci.db` and `siraloom.egg-info/`. Author config is `SayedaRehmat <siralooms@gmail.com>`. Phase A remains **PARTIAL**; this local worker failure distinction does not establish live identity, deployed tenant isolation, clinical validation, or laboratory deployment readiness.

### 2026-10-25 pin ClinGen evidence resources in profile-bound analyses

- **Finding:** profile-bound evidence execution used the persisted resource plan for ClinVar, HPO/HPOA, and population providers, but ClinGen Variant Pathogenicity and Gene-Disease Validity were discovered from a fresh organization resource plan on every worker run. A retry could therefore use a different qualified ClinGen release from the one selected at analysis preflight; the ClinGen resources also had no distinct slots in the standard profiles or canonical stage resource contract.
- **Correction:** introduced separate optional profile capabilities for ClinGen variant assertions and ClinGen gene-disease validity, resolving the existing governed `EVIDENCE` resource type through those provider-specific slots. Profile-bound worker execution now uses only the exact preflight-selected resource and qualification snapshot; multiple selected matches are rejected. Legacy analyses retain the existing organization-plan path. Standard WES/WGS GRCh37/38 profile versions were bumped from `1` to `2` so new requirements are reflected in provenance without rewriting historical analyses.
- **Regression evidence:** actual SQLAlchemy resource-profile integration tests verify approved/qualified ClinGen resources resolve into distinct plan entries with their exact IDs and execution contracts. Runtime tests verify provider filtering is applied after immutable-plan validation. Existing worker, ClinGen provenance/registry/selection, tenant-scope and resource-lifecycle tests were included.
- **Verification:** broader resource/evidence command covering 14 modules — **98 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Final profile-focused rerun — **38 passed**, same warning. Ruff import sorting on changed profile/resource modules and tests, Python compilation of changed backend/test modules, and `git diff --check` passed. An initial broad Ruff `F,I` run reported pre-existing unused imports/import-order findings in large legacy modules, including `variant.py`; no unrelated cleanup was applied. No tests used live ClinGen endpoints or laboratory datasets.
- **Status/limitations:** exact profile pinning is **VERIFIED** within deterministic SQLite/runtime tests. No independent ClinGen content benchmark, live laboratory resource qualification, PostgreSQL race test, or deployed lab execution is established. Phase A remains **PARTIAL** and laboratory release remains **BLOCKED** pending the live/deployed, scientific benchmark, and backup/restore gates in the acceptance matrix.
- **Repository status at this checkpoint:** implementation is under review on `main`; do not treat the untracked `ci.db` and `siraloom.egg-info/` as part of this change.

### 2026-10-25 constrain ClinGen profile providers to their resource class

- **Follow-up finding:** the new ClinGen profile capability slots search the shared `EVIDENCE` resource class. The profile resolver's generic unknown-provider behavior is permissive for laboratory-extensible providers, so without a capability-specific provider contract, another `EVIDENCE` provider could appear eligible for one of these ClinGen-only slots if provider preferences or matching logic changed.
- **Correction:** profile selection now explicitly binds `CLINGEN_VARIANT_ASSERTION` to provider `ClinGen Variant Pathogenicity` and `CLINGEN_GENE_DISEASE_VALIDITY` to provider `ClinGen Gene-Disease Validity`, requiring the persisted resource type to be `EVIDENCE`. Added positive and negative matcher tests.
- **Verification:** full backend suite excluding the Windows-native PDF renderer: **1,380 passed, 46 skipped**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. This run began before the capability-specific guard was added, so it is baseline regression evidence only. After the guard and its regression were present, the focused resource/profile/ClinGen suite passed **56 tests**, with the same warning. Ruff import sorting on the changed resolver, Python compilation of changed modules, and `git diff --check` passed. The full-suite run's skipped tests include unavailable integration dependencies; the PDF renderer remained excluded.
- **Status:** provider eligibility is **VERIFIED** by focused tests and exact-SHA CI run [40181513998](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/40181513998), which passed all jobs. No live ClinGen or laboratory dataset was exercised; the follow-up commit `a88f494` is on `main`.

### 2026-10-25 allow pinned ClinGen evidence slots through the durable execution guard

- **Finding:** after adding the ClinGen-specific optional capabilities to the standard profiles and evidence-stage contract, the shared `start_resource_execution()` authorization/provenance guard still allowed only the older `CLINICAL_DATABASE` and generic `GENE_DISEASE` capability names for `build_evidence`. Thus a profile could resolve the exact pinned ClinGen resources, but the production worker would reject execution before the provider query.
- **Correction:** added `CLINGEN_VARIANT_ASSERTION` and `CLINGEN_GENE_DISEASE_VALIDITY` to the `build_evidence` allowlist in `_assert_profile_bound_resource_execution`. Failure metadata now names these exact profile capabilities. Regression tests prove both resource classes pass the guard only for `build_evidence` and are denied for `annotate`.
- **Verification:** focused profile-bound execution, registry selection, runtime resource, worker recovery and ClinGen evidence suites — **60 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Added a SQLite-backed regression using the actual `Analysis`, `Resource`, `ResourceQualification`, and `ResourceExecutionRecord` tables; both ClinGen capability slots now call production `start_resource_execution()` and assert the persisted row identity, provider, stage, qualification, status and metadata. Resource execution plus profile-bound guard suites passed **22 tests**, one same warning. Python compilation and `git diff --check` passed; Ruff `--select F` passed on the changed backend execution module and tests, and import sorting passed on the profile-bound test. Ruff `--select I` on the legacy execution module reports a pre-existing local-import ordering issue at `start_resource_execution`; it was left unchanged as unrelated formatting.
- **Repository status:** core guard fix is pushed to `main` as `bd58fc7`; expanded guard test is pushed as `c8d9e0c`. Exact-SHA CI run [40181841651](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/40181841651) for `bd58fc7` and run [40181958472](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/40181958472) for `c8d9e0c` both passed all jobs. Tests do not exercise a live ClinGen query or lab resource. Phase A remains **PARTIAL**.

### 2026-10-25 execute pinned ClinGen evidence through the worker and replay

- **Findings:** the profile-bound population stage created a partial plan object without `profile_version` or `unavailable_optional`, although later stage metadata read both fields; this failed a real worker execution before evidence processing. ClinGen evidence extraction also read `gene`, `hgvs_consequences` and `hgvs` from the outer annotation JSON even though the persisted annotation contract places them under `payload.normalized`. In addition, VEP's normalized `hgvs` is an object with `c`/`p` fields, which the previous query code treated as one string representation.
- **Corrections:** profile-bound population metadata now uses the persisted resource-plan/deployment profile version and optional-resource list. ClinGen query identity now comes from the normalized annotation and extracts string HGVS values from supported string, consequence-object, and VEP `{c,p}` representations, preserving unique values.
- **Regression evidence:** extended the actual worker-loss/replay integration test with two locally qualified, profile-pinned ClinGen resources and synthetic CSV/TSV assertions. The worker executes the population stage, queries both actual provider adapters, persists evidence and the criterion-level source assertion, loses the worker after the evidence commit but before checkpoint completion, then replays without duplicate evidence or execution rows. It verifies resource identity, release, fingerprints, source assertion linkage, and profile-version provenance. Exact command: `PYTHONPATH=. DATABASE_URL=sqlite:////tmp/siraloom-clingen-recheck.db REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0 GENEBE_ENABLED=false GNOMAD_ENABLED=false .venv/bin/python -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp=/tmp/siraloom-clingen-recheck-tests -q tests/test_actual_population_stage_redelivery.py tests/test_profile_bound_resource_execution.py tests/test_resource_execution.py tests/test_clingen_provenance.py tests/test_evidence_engine.py tests/test_reanalysis_reuse_provenance.py tests/test_worker_loss_recovery_matrix.py --tb=short` — **46 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Python compilation and `git diff --check` passed. Ruff `--select F` on the touched workflow module reported seven pre-existing unused imports/variables at unrelated lines; no unrelated lint cleanup was included.
- **Status/limitations:** the local worker/provider/replay path is **VERIFIED** for deterministic synthetic resources. This is not a live ClinGen endpoint test, official dataset qualification, clinical validation, or PostgreSQL-specific race test. Exact-SHA CI [40185032642](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/40185032642) for `b9aee2f` passed all jobs. Phase A remains **PARTIAL**.

### 2026-10-25 report reference-build mismatch before sample mapping diagnostics

- **Finding:** worker input validation rejected a reference assembly mismatch, but only after checking sample mapping. A VCF with both a mismatched build and missing sample metadata therefore surfaced the less fundamental sample error first, obscuring an assembly incompatibility.
- **Correction:** validate supported assembly declarations and exact input/reference agreement immediately after artifact type/status checks, before sample cardinality and mapping. Sample-less and multisample VCFs remain rejected.
- **Fixture corrections:** recovery tests now provide the declared one-sample mapping and normalized genotype evidence required by the workflow; the lock test forwards the report type parameter. The existing safeguard was retained.
- **Verification:** five affected test modules — **42 passed**. Full backend suite excluding `tests/test_report_pdf.py` — **1,373 passed, 46 skipped**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. PDF rendering was not part of this run.
- **Status/limitation:** verified local backend regression scope. No live laboratory VCFs or production PostgreSQL deployment were tested.

### 2026-10-25 validate QC source artifact evidence

- **Finding:** the QC UI listed validated VCFs, but the API accepted any same-case analysis artifact as a QC source. Such a link could cite an invalid VCF or even a report PDF as the source for a technical QC measurement. A previously persisted bad reference also surfaced as an unhandled error on the quality read.
- **Correction:** a shared QC provenance contract now requires a same-case/current-analysis VCF or normalized VCF, accepted validation status, and a 64-character hexadecimal SHA-256. Observation writes and artifact choices enforce it; snapshot creation/report eligibility reuse it; quality reads return an explicit conflict for invalid existing references.
- **Regression coverage:** route tests reject REPORT_PDF, INVALID VCF and PENDING VCF links; lineage tests cover invalid existing observations and foreign artifact references.
- **Verification:** QC route, lineage, report generation/sign-out and reviewer integration tests — **71 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Ruff, Python compilation and diff checks passed.
- **Limitation:** artifact-source eligibility is scoped to VCF/normalized VCF because that is the current QC UI contract; external laboratory QC reports and their schemas are not integrated or qualified.

### 2026-10-25 serialize QC writes against report sign-out

- **Finding:** report sign-out locks the analysis row before checking the QC snapshot, but QC observation and assessment routes did not take that lock. On PostgreSQL, a QC observation could race with sign-out after eligibility was evaluated.
- **Correction:** QC write routes now refresh the authorized analysis with `FOR UPDATE`, using the same lock order as final report authorization. The lock is held through the route's database commit, so sign-out and QC mutation serialize on the analysis.
- **Regression coverage:** mutation route tests assert the shared row-lock request; report sign-out integration tests exercise the affected gate.
- **Verification:** focused QC authorization, artifact lineage, report sign-out and reviewer/sign-out tests — **47 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Ruff `--select F` and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Limitation:** SQLite does not implement row-level `FOR UPDATE`; this proves the routes request the intended PostgreSQL lock but is not a live PostgreSQL concurrency race test. Phase A remains partial.

### 2026-10-25 persist QC observation lineage through report sign-out

- **Finding:** QC assessments stored measurements and evaluated thresholds but did not identify the exact technical observations or artifact checksums behind those measurements. New observations could be added after assessment without forcing reassessment, and the rendered report omitted the QC basis.
- **Correction:** migration `0053_qc_observation_snapshot` adds a nullable JSON snapshot to `QCAssessment`. The assessment API captures observation identity, metric value/unit/status, source/version, metadata and timestamp, plus linked artifact identity/type/SHA-256. The artifact API accepts optional source artifact IDs and verifies same-case/current-analysis lineage. The review page offers validated source VCF and current-analysis normalized VCF choices. Clinical report eligibility compares the snapshot and latest metric values to current observations and blocks legacy null snapshots until reassessment. The report snapshot and reanalysis manifest preserve the lineage; rendered report HTML displays the QC gate, evaluated rules and observation source/artifact digest.
- **Migration behavior:** existing QC rows remain `NULL` rather than being assigned invented provenance. Such assessments require reassessment before a new clinical report can be signed out. Downgrade drops only the added column; the QC table and prior schema remain.
- **Regression coverage:** exact observation/artifact snapshot capture, cross-case rejection, sibling-analysis artifact exclusion, stale observation invalidation, report HTML provenance, migration upgrade/downgrade, and reanalysis manifest tests.
- **Verification:** `PYTHONPATH=. DATABASE_URL=sqlite:////tmp/siraloom-qc-lineage-final3.sqlite REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0 GENEBE_ENABLED=false GNOMAD_ENABLED=false .venv/bin/python -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp=/tmp/siraloom-qc-lineage-final3 -q --tb=short tests/test_m11_migrations.py tests/test_analysis_assay_qc_gate.py tests/test_m11_technical_qc.py tests/test_quality_route_authorization.py tests/test_qc_artifact_lineage.py tests/test_reporting_signout.py tests/test_reporting_contract.py tests/test_report_finalization.py tests/test_report_signout_resume.py tests/test_report_finalization_order.py tests/test_reportability_m6.py tests/test_reviewer_to_signed_report_integration.py tests/test_route_mutation_tenant_guards.py tests/test_report_artifact_commit_compensation.py tests/test_reanalysis_reproducibility_manifest.py` — **119 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Frontend session suite — **39 passed**; i18n guard and TypeScript `--noEmit` passed. Ruff `--select F`, Python compilation, and diff checks passed. The prior WSL production build did not finish final trace collection; this change has type-check evidence but no completed production build or live browser run.
- **Status/limitations:** **VERIFIED** for the listed automated code paths. Real laboratory QC source qualification, assay policy qualification, PDF rendering, live Firebase/browser and deployed lab storage remain unverified. Phase A and clinical/deployment readiness remain **PARTIAL**.

### 2026-10-25 validate legacy assay rules before analysis

- **Finding:** assay creation validation did not protect profiles already present in the database. Analysis creation only checked that `configuration.rules` was truthy, so malformed rules could enter a workflow and fail later during QC evaluation.
- **Correction:** analysis creation now runs the existing QC rule normalizer and fails closed for malformed or empty rules, as well as foreign or inactive profiles. This occurs before analysis creation and quota reservation.
- **Regression/verification:** `PYTHONPATH=. ### 2026-10-25 bind clinical sign-out to reviewed assay QC

- **Starting state:** Linux `/home/usama/SIRALOOM`, branch `main`, HEAD `914d194a1e0733c49b2e63588547c365c66913dc`, matching `origin/main` at inspection. The worktree also contained existing user changes in `docs/VARIANT_CLASS_SUPPORT.md`, this ledger, and `tests/test_vcf_tools.py`, plus untracked `ci.db`, `docs/research/`, and `siraloom.egg-info/`; those are preserved and not included in this change.
- **Finding:** clinical report eligibility did not require a reviewed, passing technical-QC assessment, analysis creation accepted unusable assay profiles, the QC review route trusted caller-supplied measurements instead of persisted observations, and the workspace did not select an assay. A report could therefore become eligible without a verifiable assay-specific QC record or could lose the QC basis after draft creation.
- **Correction:** clinical report eligibility requires an active profile bound to the case organization, a matching profile version and a reviewer-attributed PASS assessment. Assay registration validates rule configuration and allowed lifecycle state; active profiles need at least one valid rule. Analysis creation validates supplied profile ownership/state/rules. QC review values must equal the latest persisted observations. Report snapshots store the exact QC assessment and finalization rejects changed/missing/nonpassing QC. The workspace selects an available active profile and sends its ID. `ANALYTICAL` reports retain their separate existing gate.
- **Regression coverage:** tests cover invalid/unusable assay creation, fabricated QC measurements, missing/failed/stale QC, analytical report behavior, changed profile rules without a version change, and snapshot changes even when the modified measurements still pass. Analysis API gate tests cover cross-organization, inactive, empty-rule, and malformed assay profiles.
- **Verification:** Linux command `PYTHONPATH=. DATABASE_URL=sqlite:////tmp/siraloom-qc-final4.sqlite REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0 GENEBE_ENABLED=false GNOMAD_ENABLED=false .venv/bin/python -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp=/tmp/siraloom-qc-final4 -q --tb=short tests/test_analysis_assay_qc_gate.py tests/test_m11_technical_qc.py tests/test_quality_route_authorization.py tests/test_reporting_signout.py tests/test_reporting_contract.py tests/test_report_finalization.py tests/test_report_signout_resume.py tests/test_report_finalization_order.py tests/test_reportability_m6.py tests/test_reviewer_to_signed_report_integration.py tests/test_route_mutation_tenant_guards.py tests/test_report_artifact_commit_compensation.py` — **101 passed**. Frontend `npm run test:session` — **39 passed**; `npm run build` passed i18n, compile, and type checking but stalled in final trace collection under WSL, then was interrupted. Ruff `--select F` on changed backend/test files, Python compilation, and `git diff --check` passed. No browser/Firebase/deployment test was run.
- **Status/limitations:** implementation and listed backend/frontend session test scopes are **VERIFIED**; full production build is PARTIAL, real assay QC policy qualification and deployed workflow are NOT ESTABLISHED. This is not clinical validation or a production-readiness claim. Overall Phase A remains **PARTIAL**.

### 2026-10-24 retire unsupported reportability priorities

- **Finding:** the default reportability policy mapped ACMG/AMP classifications to report/do-not-report decisions and hard-coded numeric priority scores/bands. Pathogenicity classification is not itself a reportability decision; reportability depends on the laboratory's test scope, indication, policy and case context. The model's database defaults (`0` and `ROUTINE`) could also fabricate a priority for rows that omitted those fields.
- **Scientific basis:** ACMG/AMP defines variant classification categories and evidence criteria ([Richards et al. 2015](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25741868/)); ACMG constitutional NGS laboratory standards require reporting policies to be defined in relation to the test and its limitations ([Deignan et al. 2021](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41436-021-01139-4)). This change makes no claim that a replacement reportability algorithm has been clinically validated.
- **Correction:** default proposals now remain `REVIEW` for every classification and explain that a lab-approved policy must consider indication, scope and case context. The arbitrary score/band are no longer produced or exposed through report/review APIs, generated report data, PDF text or current UI. Model columns are nullable; migration `0052_remove_unvalidated_reportability_priorities` removes their database defaults while preserving historical values. Historical export/reproducibility fields are explicitly labeled as legacy unvalidated values. Downgrade is intentionally blocked because v2 decisions have no scientifically valid scores to reconstruct.
- **Regression coverage:** classification-independent review proposal, API/report/UI projections, retention of historical priority values, nullable/default-free schema, and migration downgrade isolation.
- **Verification:** focused reportability/review/signout/migration suite — **100 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. The initial full non-integration run had **1,311 passed, 17 skipped, 28 deselected, 1 failed** because the phenotype migration test attempted to downgrade across the new intentionally irreversible `0052`. That test now upgrades only through phenotype migration `0050`, testing its own downgrade behavior; the follow-up migration test uses a distinct reportability version to test omitted-field defaults. Final full non-integration suite: **1,312 passed, 17 skipped, 28 deselected**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Final focused migration tests: **15 passed**, same warning. Ruff `--select F` on affected Python files and `git diff --check` passed. Frontend i18n validation, session tests (**34/34**) and production build passed; the frontend was unchanged after that build.
- **Status/limitations:** removal of unsupported default scoring is **VERIFIED** for the code and stated automated checks. Reportability still requires an authorized human decision; no lab-specific configurable policy is implemented by this change. Phase A, scientific validation and laboratory deployment remain **PARTIAL**.
- **Release gates:** commit `5ccbb72ae13d3a7954fe1ceb33d51142c14b81b3` is verified on remote `main`. ## 2026-10-20 case-assigned reanalysis notification and change-governance fix

- **Starting repository state:** branch `main`, commit `86008358caae94898ceaedf3be9449e55e9936b6`; tracked working tree clean. Preserved unrelated untracked `siraloom.egg-info/` and the user backup artifact.
- **Confirmed defects:** case-context and resource-release reanalysis notifications were sent to every active organization member, including members without a read grant for the case. A revoked/stale notification then caused the entire notification inbox request to return 404. In addition, a case-writing role could register organization-wide resource-change events; supplied resource IDs were not checked against tenant, kind, or name; resource-change registration had no human audit record; and the response exposed individual candidate IDs.
- **Correction:** a shared recipient query now selects only active assigned case readers plus current organization administrators/lab directors/platform administrators. Both case-change and resource-change notification creation use it. The inbox filters inaccessible case notifications while retaining strict lineage validation. Resource-change registration is limited to governed resource-management roles, validates UUID and tenant/global resource identity, records the authenticated actor in the audit log, commits the audit with registration, and returns only an aggregate count.
- **Transaction correction:** `detect_change` now supports caller-managed commit; the API writes candidates, notifications, and the human audit event in one transaction and rolls back all of them if audit persistence fails. Existing internal callers retain the default commit behavior. A failure-injection regression verifies rollback is requested before any commit.
- **Regression evidence:** combined case-change/reanalysis/API/resource-adoption tests: **24 passed**; notification revocation tests: **8 passed**. Broad backend: `pytest -q --ignore=tests/test_report_pdf.py` (**1,047 passed, 40 skipped**, 1 Starlette/httpx deprecation warning). Skips include unconfigured PostgreSQL and unavailable BCFtools; PDF renderer excluded for unavailable Windows-native WeasyPrint library. `git diff --check` passed.
- **Scope / limitations:** these tests verify SQLite/unit/API behavior, not deployed tenant isolation, PostgreSQL concurrency, or production notification delivery. Phase A remains PARTIAL; live Firebase/browser, deployed two-laboratory API/artifact isolation, real-storage reviewer-to-signout recovery, and scientific/deployment evidence remain outstanding.

## 2026-10-20 GVCF and structural-record routing boundary

- **Production decision path:** after source artifact retrieval, `run_variant_analysis` profiles then partitions records with `route_vcf_records` before reference lookup. Sequence-resolved SNVs/indels (including a GVCF concrete ALT only when every sample has a complete genotype that does not call `<NON_REF>`/`<*>`) proceed to the pinned reference package and `normalize_vcf_with_bcftools`. Pure GVCF reference blocks, wildcard-called or unresolved GVCF variants, symbolic SVs, breakends, and `*` spanning-deletion records are persisted as separate deterministic review artifacts with source artifact id/hash, routing version, record count and `NOT_ANALYZED` state. If no sequence variant remains, normalization becomes `REQUIRES_REVIEW`, rather than claiming successful analysis or labeling a valid but unsupported record malformed. If mixed input has sequence variants, those variants may proceed, while report eligibility prevents signout until all preserved records have been interpreted. Annotation can subsequently block independently when its provider is unavailable.
- **Scientific/tool basis:** VCF v4.3 describes gVCF reference-confidence blocks using `<*>`/`<NON_REF>` and `END`, and breakends use mate/orientation representation ([official VCF specifications](https://samtools.github.io/hts-specs/VCFv4.3.pdf)). BCFtools 1.19 release notes describe `bcftools view -A` as removing unseen alleles such as `<*>`/`<NON_REF>` at variant sites, while `bcftools norm` handles reference-aware sequence normalization; these operations do not themselves interpret reference blocks or provide a complete SV workflow ([official BCFtools release notes](https://github.com/samtools/bcftools/blob/develop/NEWS), [BCFtools manual](https://samtools.github.io/bcftools/bcftools)). Therefore the code uses `view -A` only for wildcard alleles proven uncalled from complete sample GTs and preserves reference blocks and SV representations for a future appropriate workflow. No assembly conversion or SV interpretation is performed.
- **Positive and negative regression evidence:** the production-wrapper SQLite tests exercise valid GVCF reference block + concrete variant, sequence deletion with a contextually supported spanning `*`, valid `<DEL>` with `END`, paired breakends, malformed symbolic SV lacking span, and a reference-mismatch failure at the normalization boundary. They assert durable sidecar and normalized artifacts, source linkage, routing counts, stored-file SHA-256, variant indexing, and blocked/failed/review states. Focused command `$py -m pytest tests/test_unsupported_vcf_worker_routing.py tests/test_vcf_validation.py tests/test_vcf_tools.py tests/test_reporting_signout.py -q -p no:cacheprovider` passed **44**, **5 skipped**. Broad command `$py -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider --ignore=tests/test_report_pdf.py` passed **986**, **40 skipped**, in **61.27s**. `$py` was `C:\Users\usama\AppData\Local\Temp\siraloom-verify-86f7e0b\Scripts\python.exe`. Python compilation and `git diff --check` pass (Git reports only CRLF conversion notices).
- **Verified CI and remaining checks:** exact-SHA run [39395036911](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/39395036911) for `300eae3` passed. The backend ran actual BCFtools 1.19 golden tests and the full suite (**1,046 passed, 27 skipped**); PostgreSQL integration passed (**26 passed**); frontend, Python compilation, startup-script validation, backend Docker image build, and container BCFtools 1.19 assertion all succeeded. Regression coverage verifies source immutability and exact text/checksum preservation for valid GVCF block, symbolic deletion, paired BND records, and contextual spanning deletion; the actual BCFtools golden verifies allele-indexed outputs for a concrete GVCF variant. Local exact-binary/PostgreSQL execution is unavailable, so CI provides those checks. Full laboratory deployment, external storage restoration, deployed cross-tenant checks, live identity journey, and independent truth-set benchmarking remain unverified.
- **Files in this worktree:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; `backend/app/domain/vcf_tools.py`; `backend/app/domain/vcf_validation.py`; `backend/app/reporting/service.py`; `backend/app/workflows/variant.py`; `configs/bioinformatics/bcftools-environment.yml`; `docker/backend.Dockerfile`; `tests/test_bcftools_normalization.py`; `tests/test_reporting_signout.py`; `tests/test_unsupported_vcf_worker_routing.py`; `tests/test_vcf_tools.py`; `tests/test_vcf_validation.py`.
- **Git/release status:** implementation and regression tests are pushed on `main` at `300eae3`; exact-SHA CI is green. The unrelated untracked `siraloom.egg-info/` and backup artifact are preserved and excluded. This scoped VCF routing workstream now has passing production-wrapper, actual BCFtools 1.19, PostgreSQL CI, frontend, and image-build evidence, but does not complete Phase A or establish scientific/clinical/production readiness. Continue with the broader Phase A blockers and outstanding deployment/benchmark evidence.

## 2026-10-19 report sign-out status integrity

- **Finding:** report finalization stamped `final_result.release_state` as `CLINICALLY_RELEASED` solely after an authorized user signed out. The same report stated that clinical use requires laboratory-specific validation, and the system has no evidence-backed laboratory validation qualification gate. The PDF renderer also kept saying sign-out had not yet occurred after a report was signed.
- **Correction:** finalization now records `AUTHORIZED_SIGNOUT` and separately records `clinical_use_status=LABORATORY_VALIDATION_REQUIRED`. Draft reports retain `NOT_FOR_CLINICAL_RELEASE` and expose the validation-required state. Rendered report text distinguishes an unsigned draft from an authorized sign-out while keeping laboratory-specific validation and quality/regulatory controls explicit. It does not assert that clinical validation was achieved.
- **Scientific basis:** ACMG/AMP sequence-variant guidance defines interpretation recommendations for clinical diagnostic laboratory use ([Richards et al., 2015](https://www.nature.com/articles/gim201530)); ACMG laboratory technical standards describe minimum criteria for accurate/reliable diagnostic testing and call for laboratory-specific validation or applicable published support ([ACMG Genetics Laboratory Standards](https://www.acmg.net/genetics-laboratory-standards/)). Human authorization is therefore not itself evidence that this software or its laboratory workflow has been validated.
- **Regression coverage:** finalization tests assert the persisted authorized-signout state and the signed HTML notice; reportability tests assert draft status and draft-only wording.
- **Verification:** focused report, reportability, and integrated reviewer-to-signout tests passed **35**. Full backend suite excluding `tests/test_report_pdf.py` passed **964**, with **39 skipped**, one Starlette/httpx deprecation warning, in **48.85s**. Skips include 26 PostgreSQL tests without `DATABASE_URL` and 13 bcftools tests without the executable. The Windows-native PDF renderer test was excluded because WeasyPrint's native libraries are unavailable. `git diff --check` and Python syntax parsing passed.
- **Git state:** committed and pushed to `main` as [`c68e0f2`](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/commit/c68e0f24325f950a9f13e5f1a778fee8eb0d055a); local and remote `main` were verified at the same SHA. Exact-SHA CI is not verified.
- **Release status:** this corrects a misleading label; it does not satisfy independent validation, live/deployed sign-out, object-storage recovery, or laboratory qualification. Phase A remains **PARTIAL**.

## 2026-10-19 readiness probe semantics

- **Finding:** `/api/v1/ready` returned HTTP 200 for dependency failures while its body said `not_ready`. Load balancers and deployment systems that trust HTTP status could continue routing requests to an unusable instance.
- **Correction:** the endpoint now sets HTTP 503 for any failed required check and keeps HTTP 200 for a ready result. Production Docker Compose and Render probes now target `/api/v1/ready`, while the separate `/api/v1/health` endpoint remains dependency-independent liveness. The response continues to include stable redacted check identifiers.
- **Regression coverage:** readiness tests assert HTTP 200 on a ready configuration and HTTP 503 for missing required Firebase and database failures. A deployment-config regression asserts Compose and Render use readiness rather than liveness.
- **Verification:** API behavior passed run #392261; the deployment-probe adjustment and regression passed run #392269 on `9a81ac1111c28aa06ad220ef89a1bc267489216d` (backend, PostgreSQL integration, frontend). Local `compileall` and `git diff --check` pass. Local pytest is unavailable because the bundled Python runtime lacks pytest.
- **Limit:** this establishes source and CI semantics only. The deployed health-check configuration and actual laboratory readiness behavior remain unverified. Phase A remains PARTIAL.

## 2026-10-19 VCF header and FORMAT conformance

- **Finding:** strict validation did not compare each data row's tab-column count with the `#CHROM` header. It also accepted `GT` in a non-first FORMAT position and rejected standards-compliant omission of trailing per-sample FORMAT fields. Upload validation used the weaker parser, so malformed sample cardinality could be recorded as valid until worker execution.
- **Correction:** strict validation now requires record/header column cardinality to match, enforces GT-first when GT is present, rejects empty FORMAT values, permits omitted trailing FORMAT values, and runs at upload intake before the VCF is marked valid.
- **Scientific basis:** the official [HTS VCF v4.5 specification](https://github.com/samtools/hts-specs/blob/master/VCFv4.5.tex) states there are eight fixed fields followed by sample FORMAT data, GT must be first when present, and trailing FORMAT fields may be omitted. The implementation deliberately continues to accept only VCFv4.2 and VCFv4.3; it does not claim v4.5 support.
- **Regression / verification:** VCF strict-validation and intake suites: **25 passed** locally. Manual checks confirmed trailing omissions accepted and row-cardinality/GT-order errors rejected. Python `compileall` and `git diff --check` passed. CI for exact code SHA `0eb61a49f2f2f0e115f56436cb5640e05350599e` remains unverified because GitHub's unauthenticated Actions API is rate limited.
- **Limit:** this is format validation only; no reference truth-set, annotation, or clinical benchmark was run. VCFv4.4/v4.5 remain outside the declared intake scope. Phase A remains PARTIAL.

## 2026-10-19 continuation — resource staging duplicate-dispatch fence

- **Finding:** `execute_resource_staging` treated a `QUEUING` dispatch older than 120 seconds as definitively abandoned, and did not apply the same age check to `PUBLISHED`. A slow broker/backend or a stale result record could therefore cause a second task ID while the original task might still run against the same staging destination. Celery `PENDING` and `UNKNOWN`, a result-backend error, or a missing/invalid timestamp do not prove a dispatch is dead.
- **Correction:** an old `QUEUING` or `PUBLISHED` dispatch is now replaced only when its task ID exists and the Celery result backend explicitly reports `FAILURE` or `REVOKED`. `RUNNING`, recent tasks, `PENDING`, `UNKNOWN`, unavailable result backend, and invalid/missing timestamps remain idempotent and return the existing dispatch. A definitively dead task can receive a new dispatch generation.
- **Regression / verification:** helper coverage checks stale pending/unknown/failure/revoked, active RUNNING, result-backend exception, and invalid timestamps. Route regression ages an actual persisted PUBLISHED row, makes Celery report PENDING, retries the endpoint, and asserts no extra task ID is published. `pytest tests/test_resource_staging_api.py tests/test_resource_staging_dispatch_fence.py tests/test_resource_staging_orchestration.py -q -p no:cacheprovider` passed **37 tests**. Full backend suite excluding `tests/test_report_pdf.py` passed **936 passed, 36 skipped, 1 warning in 50.77s** before the final test-only strengthening. Python compilation and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Scope limitation:** an old `QUEUING` state with a PENDING/UNKNOWN result remains visible and requires operator investigation; automatically replacing it would risk duplicate resource acquisition. This code does not claim deployed broker recovery or live resource staging verification.
- **Status:** Phase A remains PARTIAL. Continue route/worker audit; live auth, two-tenant deployment, and real-storage reviewer-to-signout/recovery acceptance are not verified.

## 2026-10-18 continuation — analysis resource-snapshot tenant validation

- **Finding:** the shared `snapshot_analysis_resources` routine trusted resource IDs on reference configuration, population observations, annotations, evidence and existing snapshots without checking organization ownership. The production provenance-export stage calls it immediately before changing the analysis to `SUCCEEDED`; the manual snapshot API calls the same routine. A foreign organization-scoped resource could therefore be pinned into another lab's analysis provenance.
- **Finding:** in addition, the manual `POST /analyses/{id}/provenance/snapshot` endpoint allowed a `read_only` principal to create durable snapshot rows and recorded no audit event.
- **Correction:** snapshot creation now resolves the analysis case, permits globally governed resources or resources owned by that case's organization, rejects missing or foreign referenced resources, validates previously persisted snapshot links on idempotent calls, and scopes ACMG resource matching to global/current-organization rows. Invalid reference resource IDs fail explicitly. The manual endpoint now requires a case-write role and records its provenance snapshot in the same transaction.
- **Regression / verification:** added a SQLite regression with a cross-tenant reference resource and verified it fails without persisting snapshot rows; added an API regression proving `read_only` cannot invoke snapshot creation. ## 2026-10-18 continuation — bound and runtime resource tenant fence

- **Finding:** the worker runtime's singular and plural preflight resolvers loaded selected resources by ID and verified the execution contract, but did not verify the resource owner against the analysis case. The preflight planner and `get_active_resource_for_organization` likewise trusted an `OrganizationResourceBinding` row's tenant while resolving its `resource_id`. A corrupted or stale cross-organization binding could therefore make preflight appear runnable or return another lab's resource to a caller.
- **Correction:** planner candidate loading now retains only global resources or resources owned by the requested organization. Both runtime resolvers resolve the analysis case and fail with `RESOURCE_TENANT_MISMATCH` before provider execution if the selected resource belongs to another organization. The active-resource domain accessor returns no resource for a missing or foreign-owned binding target. Missing analysis-case lineage fails explicitly.
- **Regression / verification:** planner coverage proves own-organization and global resources are retained while foreign and missing resources are excluded; singular and plural runtime tests reject foreign resources before provider execution; the registry test injects a cross-tenant binding and confirms the shared accessor returns `None`. The combined planner/runtime/fallback/registry/preflight/reanalysis/authorization command passed **101 tests** before the accessor addition. The final resource-registry/planner/runtime/fallback/preflight group passed **52 tests** after it. One Starlette/httpx deprecation warning was emitted in each run.
- **Current state:** planner/runtime and active-resource accessor fixes were committed and pushed to `main` as `26431e8`. The updated resource-registry/planner/runtime/fallback/preflight tests passed **52**. CI for the commit is not verified. Continue worker/resource-boundary audit. Phase A remains PARTIAL; deployed cross-tenant verification and live identity/report-signout recovery are not available here.

## 2026-10-18 continuation — snapshot duplicate-lineage validation

- **Finding:** annotation and evidence resource snapshots deduplicated records before validating every row's optional `resource_id`; a later duplicate could hide a foreign-tenant or missing resource. The deduplication key also omitted the resolved resource checksum and could collapse distinct release identities.
- **Correction:** every annotation/evidence resource reference is now resolved and ownership-checked before deduplication. Snapshot identity includes the resolved checksum, and rows that map to the same durable snapshot key but disagree on resource identity/provider fail explicitly rather than selecting one silently.
- **Regression / verification:** added a duplicate annotation regression where a first unregistered row and a later same-label row reference a foreign resource; the snapshot now fails closed. `pytest tests/test_reanalysis_resource_snapshot_pins.py tests/test_reanalysis.py tests/test_resource_registry.py tests/test_resource_execution_plan_tenant_scope.py tests/test_profile_runtime_resources.py -q -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp <temp>` passed **34 tests** with one Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Python syntax compilation and `git diff --check` remain to be run before checkpointing.
- **Current state:** committed and pushed as `bdd3067` within the `c1d2eb0` main checkpoint. The push initially failed with DNS resolution, then succeeded on retry. The unrelated backup artifact remains untouched. Phase A remains PARTIAL. Continue the worker resource-lineage audit.

## 2026-10-18 current continuation — stale membership tenant boundary

- **Finding:** verified source inspection confirmed that self-onboarding may re-provision a user with no active membership into another organization while retaining the old revoked/suspended membership. The old tenant's membership roster joined that historical row to the user's current global profile, disclosing current email/display name. Its membership mutation endpoint also updated global `User.role` and `User.status` through the old membership, allowing a former tenant administrator to disrupt the user's current account.
- **Correction:** membership rosters now return a user only when `User.organization_id` still matches the roster tenant. Membership mutation takes the subject-level provisioning lock, then locks and refreshes the target user before checking its current tenant and locking/updating the membership; a stale cross-tenant historical row is hidden as not found. Refreshing the locked ORM row closes the race where provisioning moves the identity after the first lookup. This prevents the former tenant from changing global account fields.
- **Regression coverage:** added a SQLite test for a revoked membership retained after identity reprovisioning, and a mutation regression proving the former tenant cannot change the current account. Updated lock-order coverage for the new ownership check.
- **Verification:** the full Python test suite excluding only the Windows-native PDF renderer test passed **917 passed, 36 skipped**, including the membership roster/mutation regressions. Python syntax compilation and `git diff --check` passed. No CI result exists for this working-tree change.
- **Status / next:** both this tenant-boundary fix and the retry-exhaustion guard remain uncommitted on local `main` because the sandbox denies `.git` writes. Run focused auth/membership and Celery retry tests in a Python-capable environment, inspect the full diff, then commit/push when Git metadata write access is available. Phase A remains PARTIAL; live identity/browser, deployed two-tenant API/artifact isolation, and real-storage signout/recovery remain outstanding.

## 2026-10-18 analysis-start lifecycle and capacity recovery (working tree)

- **Finding:** `POST /analyses/{id}/start` performed resource/capacity preflight before validating the analysis lifecycle. On a capacity failure it unconditionally set the analysis to BLOCKED and returned before the durable dispatch state guard. A duplicate start could therefore overwrite SUCCEEDED/RUNNING state when capacity had changed; a previously capacity-blocked analysis also had no explicit transition back to a startable state when capacity recovered.
- **Correction:** the start route now locks and refreshes the analysis before deciding. QUEUED/RUNNING retries return the existing dispatch without re-running preflight; terminal/human-gated states reject with 409. BLOCKED can re-enter preflight only when its persisted resource plan or capacity step identifies a recoverable preflight block. Once a capacity-only block clears, the route restores CREATED before the existing outbox dispatch path.
- **Regression coverage:** added route tests for terminal-state rejection, queued/running idempotency without capacity rechecks, and a SQLite capacity-block recovery through durable dispatch creation.
- **Verification:** the capacity/recovery route suite passed **9 tests**, and the full Python suite excluding only the Windows-native PDF renderer test passed **917 passed, 36 skipped**. Python syntax compilation and `git diff --check` passed. No CI evidence exists for this working-tree fix.
- **Next:** check PostgreSQL concurrent start behavior and continue API/worker audit; commit/push when `.git` writes are permitted. Phase A remains PARTIAL with live Firebase/browser, deployed tenant isolation and real-storage report recovery unverified.

## 2026-10-17 pedigree observation lineage and interpretation guard

4. Complete the evidence, review, reportability, artifact generation, signout, amendment, and audit-lineage trace. Verify each critical guard in route and service tests.
5. Complete frontend contract, stale-state, loading/error, accessibility, navigation, and authenticated-download review; run available frontend tests/build and a real browser journey if configured infrastructure exists.
6. Complete the reanalysis and resource-release lifecycle audit, including failure and duplicate-delivery cases.
7. Run the Phase 1 acceptance gate and update every matrix row with actual evidence; keep any unavailable external checks explicitly BLOCKED/NOT RUN.

## Phase decision

**Phase 1: PARTIAL.** The current code has broad automated coverage and several previously repaired workflow controls, but a complete source-level trace and authenticated end-to-end browser-to-persistence-to-signout verification have not been demonstrated. This is not a clinical validation, regulatory, or production-readiness determination.

## 2026-10-15 workspace case-switch privacy and stale-response correction

- **Finding:** changing the selected case left the previous case's analysis, specimens, variants, review, report, export, artifacts, and audit entries rendered until the new case request completed. In-flight polling and detail requests could also write the previous case's data after a switch.
- **Correction:** clear case-scoped state when selection changes; render only a loading/retry surface until `caseWorkspace.case_id` matches the selected case; cancel case-detail requests on cleanup; ignore stale responses from analysis, variant, audit, export, and artifact reads. Added a pure readiness-policy regression test.
- **Verification:** frontend `npm run test:session` passed 14 tests; `npm run validate:i18n` passed; `git diff --check` passed. CI run #1542 passed backend, PostgreSQL integration, and frontend jobs for prior commit `a270237`; the current UI correction still requires its own push-triggered CI run. Local Next build cannot run because `frontend/node_modules` is absent.
- **Checkpoint:** correction was committed and pushed as `c409f68`; GitHub Actions run #1543 passed all three jobs. Phase A remains PARTIAL: case switching is guarded in code and helper tests, but interactive browser account/tenant switching, remaining frontend surfaces, and all remaining Phase A workflow checks are still outstanding.

## 2026-10-15 audit timeline case-switch privacy correction

- **Finding:** the standalone audit page retained the prior case's events in the timeline while another case was selected; overlapping requests could allow an older response to overwrite the current selection.
- **Correction:** associate loaded events with their case identity, hide the timeline unless that identity matches the current selection, clear prior events on selection changes, and ignore canceled or superseded audit/list responses.
- **Verification:** frontend `npm run test:session` passed 14 tests; `npm run validate:i18n` and `git diff --check` passed. The shared case-identity readiness policy test covers the visible-data guard. This page change is not yet included in a production build or CI run.
- **Checkpoint:** correction was committed and pushed as `5519751`; GitHub Actions run #1544 passed all three jobs. Reports were then separately corrected in `c914c44`; run #1545 is in progress. Phase A remains PARTIAL; review still requires the same stale-response audit, and no browser-based journey has been demonstrated.

## 2026-10-15 report-page analysis-scope correction (working tree)

- **Finding:** the Reports page continued rendering decision and report rows from the previous analysis while another analysis ID was selected; racing requests could overwrite the newly selected analysis's data.
- **Correction:** results and selected decision are rendered only when their loaded analysis ID matches the selected ID; overlapping reads are generation-checked; reportability and report-generation actions remain disabled until the selected analysis data is loaded. Changing analyses also clears the prior decision selection, rationale, and disposition.
- **Verification:** frontend `npm run test:session` passed 15 tests, including the analysis-identity readiness policy; `npm run validate:i18n` passed. CI run #1545 caught a TypeScript error in the bootstrap effect cleanup. The scope error was fixed and pushed as `bc22d41`; run #1546 is in progress.
- **Checkpoint:** report-scope correction is committed as `c914c44`, and the build hotfix is committed as `bc22d41`. The review-page correction remains in the working tree and requires its own build verification. Phase A remains PARTIAL.

## 2026-10-15 clinical review analysis and variant context correction (working tree)

- **Finding:** changing analyses or selected variants could leave a previous clinical dossier, governance record, pedigree/segregation data, or reviewer rationale visible or writable while new review detail was loading. Late queue/detail responses could also replace newer selections.
- **Correction:** queue visibility is bound to its loaded analysis ID; dossier visibility additionally requires the selected variant and returned review bundle to match. Queue, review, governance, and family reads ignore superseded requests. Analysis changes reset case-specific reviewer edits, and variant changes clear variant-specific review data and rationale.
- **Verification:** frontend `npm run test:session` passed 16 tests (including analysis readiness and identity-key policy); `npm run validate:i18n` and `git diff --check` passed. The Next production build is unavailable locally because `frontend/node_modules` is absent; push-triggered CI will verify the review-page TypeScript/build integration.
- **Checkpoint:** review-page and Firebase identity remount corrections are in the working tree pending commit/push. CI #1546 passed the preceding report hotfix; current changes still need their own build run. Phase A remains PARTIAL and no browser-based reviewer/account-switch journey has been run.

## 2026-10-15 account-switch in-memory workspace isolation correction (working tree)

- **Finding:** auth-state changes cleared localStorage context but did not remount protected route components. On a direct Firebase UID change, case/report/review data already held in React component state could remain rendered for the next account.
- **Correction:** key the authenticated route subtree by a namespaced Firebase UID (and a distinct signed-out key), forcing route-local state to be discarded when the identity changes. Added a policy regression for distinct identities and signout.
- **Verification:** frontend behavior tests now pass 16 tests and localization validation passes. This app-shell integration has not yet been covered by a real Firebase/browser account switch; production build will be checked by CI after push.
- **Checkpoint:** code and test are pending commit/push with the Review page correction. Phase A remains PARTIAL.

## 2026-10-15 browser account-context checkpoint (local, pending push)

- **Finding:** the app stored `siraloom.case_id`, `siraloom.case_identifier`, and `siraloom.analysis_id` globally in browser localStorage. `AppShell` did not clear them when Firebase identity changed or signed out. Server-side tenant checks prevented these IDs alone from granting access, but a different account inherited stale context and UI requests.
- **Change:** introduced `syncWorkspaceContext`, called from Firebase auth-state changes. It clears case/analysis context on signout or UID change, preserves it for the same UID, and tolerates restricted browser storage. Signout failures now display an English/Arabic alert. The `test:session` command now includes case artifact and account-context tests.

### 2026-10-17 worker input artifact lineage guard (working tree)

- **Finding:** `run_variant_analysis` trusted `analysis.configuration["input_artifact_id"]` when retrieving an artifact for a background task. Unlike the API enqueue path, the worker did not independently enforce that the artifact belonged to the analysis case, nor that a modern primary VCF was ingestion-validated for the analysis assembly. Persisted configuration drift or direct task delivery could therefore make the worker read a foreign case's bytes or an invalid artifact.
- **Correction:** the worker now checks case ownership before materializing bytes. Modern `VCF` artifacts must satisfy the same `validate_analysis_input_artifact` contract as analysis creation. Unsupported artifact types and explicitly invalid legacy `SOURCE_VCF` artifacts fail closed; historical `SOURCE_VCF` analyses otherwise remain supported and pass through the existing strict on-disk VCF validator.
- **Regression:** added four parameterized cases in `tests/test_annotation_workflow_wrapper.py` for cross-case artifact, unsupported normalized artifact, failed ingestion status, and reference assembly mismatch. Each asserts rejection before the storage materializer is invoked, and the analysis enters its existing failed state.
- **Verification:** Python syntax compilation passed. After installing the minimum test stack into temporary storage and using a workspace-local pytest temp base, the worker wrapper tests passed within a combined **76-test** focused run. `git diff --check` passed. Full backend suite on `fb21c38`: 905 passed, 37 skipped, with only the Windows-native WeasyPrint failure.

### 2026-10-17 reanalysis trigger-to-recomputation integrity

- **Finding:** `/reanalysis/change-events` accepted independent `resource_kind` and `trigger_type` values. The trigger selects the earliest recomputation step, so an inconsistent pair could schedule work from the wrong step and reuse stale annotations, population observations, or evidence. Persisted candidates were also trusted without verifying that their resource kind and stored recomputation step matched the governed trigger.
- **Correction:** the API now accepts only the resource-kind/trigger pairs in the existing governed mapping. Candidate listing, notification lineage, and execution verify the event's resource kind/trigger pair and the candidate's expected affected step before disclosing or executing it.
- **Regression coverage:** API tests reject an inconsistent pair before change detection and accept a governed pair; candidate tests reject mismatched event resource kind and stale recomputation step.
- **Verification:** reanalysis API/domain, tenant authorization, case-change lineage, and new change-event API suites passed **74**; Python syntax compilation and `git diff --check` passed. This is a targeted regression run, not a full suite.
- **Status:** committed and pushed to `main` as `9ce8e39`. Targeted tests passed **74**. CI #1648/#1649 were in progress at the last authenticated check; current status could not be refreshed because the approval review hit the account usage limit. Phase A remains PARTIAL. Browser/Firebase, deployed two-lab storage isolation, and real-storage signout/recovery evidence remain unverified.

### 2026-10-18 stale retry-exhaustion callback guard (working tree)

- **Finding:** the Celery transient-retry exhaustion terminalizer loaded an analysis by ID and unconditionally changed it to FAILED. A delayed callback could overwrite a newer state or a newer dispatch generation after a review/retry transition.
- **Correction:** the terminalizer now locks the analysis row, only proceeds while the analysis is still RUNNING, and—when invoked by the Celery task—requires the task ID to match the analysis's current `queue_task_id`. The task passes its own request ID into the terminalizer.
- **Regression coverage:** added stale-state and stale-dispatch-generation cases that assert the row lock and verify no step mutation is attempted; the existing task-level test now asserts task-ID propagation.
- **Verification:** `git diff --check` passed. The focused pytest command could not start because the bundled Python runtime has no `pytest` module; the normal `python.exe` resolves to an inaccessible Microsoft Store alias. The bundled interpreter does run `py_compile` successfully for changed Python files. No behavioral test pass is claimed for this working-tree change.
- **Status / next:** changes are currently uncommitted on local `main`; they have not been pushed or CI-verified. The GitHub approval path is blocked by the account usage limit until its displayed reset time, and prior Git index writes were denied by the workspace permission boundary. Phase A remains PARTIAL. Continue the route/worker audit when execution permits; live Firebase/browser identity, deployed two-tenant API/artifact isolation, and real-storage reviewer-to-signout recovery still require configured environments.

### 2026-10-18 normalization-manifest consumers fail-closed (working tree)

- **Finding:** several consumers bypassed the authoritative input-membership guard. The review queue trusted `Annotation.analysis_id`; the variants API and complete variant report fell back to annotations whenever the manifest yielded no parseable IDs; report eligibility and draft construction trusted every analysis-scoped classification. A malformed/empty present manifest could therefore expose/report a variant absent from normalized input and could allow an inconsistent classification into a draft/signout gate. A valid zero-record input has no partition rows, so treating every no-partition analysis as legacy would also enable fallback.
- **Correction:** review-queue rows are checked against the shared manifest membership set and inconsistent persisted lineage returns HTTP 409. Variant listing and complete JSON/CSV reports use the shared membership helper; a completed streaming normalization step with zero partitions is treated as an authoritative empty manifest, while legacy fallback applies only when no current-manifest marker exists. Reportability proposal/finalization, report eligibility and draft construction reject classifications and reportable secondary findings absent from the analysis input; the API returns 409 for a persisted lineage conflict. The final reportability gate also rejects orphan decisions and requires exact classification coverage. Worker manual-review, classification-review, and reportability gates require annotations/classifications to match the normalization manifest exactly.
- **Regression coverage:** added review-queue, list/report legacy-vs-empty/malformed/zero-record manifest, reportability proposal/finalization/coverage/orphan-decision and route-conflict, report-generation/signout-eligibility, and worker human-gate mismatch regressions.
- **Verification:** current full suite excluding `tests/test_report_pdf.py` passed **917 passed, 36 skipped**, including the manifest, reportability, report generation/signout, and worker gate regressions. The unfiltered suite's remaining failure is WeasyPrint unable to load Windows native `libgobject-2.0-0`. Python syntax compilation and `git diff --check` passed. No CI result is claimed. Git index writes remain denied in this task's current permission profile; changes remain uncommitted and unpushed.
- **Next:** continue auditing analysis-scoped worker stage inputs and remaining exports. Phase A remains PARTIAL; live Firebase/browser, deployed two-tenant API/artifact isolation, and integrated real-storage reviewer-to-signout/recovery are still unverified.

### 2026-10-18 annotation response checkpoint completion ordering (pushed in 3385b69)

- **Finding:** the annotation worker committed its `ResourceExecutionRecord` as `SUCCEEDED` immediately after receiving the provider payload, before the response artifact and `RESPONSE_PERSISTED` batch checkpoint were durably committed. A crash or artifact-storage failure in that interval left a false success record and no replayable response checkpoint.
- **Correction:** the worker now persists and commits the provider response artifact plus batch checkpoint first, then marks and commits the resource execution as `SUCCEEDED`. Recovery closes abandoned `STARTED` resource execution rows as `INTERRUPTED` with an explicit completion timestamp and audit count. Existing replay handling continues to mark a new execution successful only after it has loaded a validated durable response checkpoint. Annotation batches also reject inconsistent per-record provider, version, request fingerprint, or response fingerprint provenance instead of recording batch-level provenance from only the first record.
- **Regression coverage:** extended `tests/test_actual_annotation_stage_redelivery.py`, which executes the production workflow body and Celery wrapper with deterministic provider/resource adapters, to simulate worker death immediately after the response artifact/checkpoint commit, verify the execution remains `STARTED`, redeliver and replay the response without a second provider call, then simulate worker death after annotation-row commit and verify subsequent reconciliation completes without duplication. The final persistence assertions require one `INTERRUPTED` attempt and one `SUCCEEDED` attempt. Added a unit regression for consistent request/response provenance across batch records. The test no longer requires bcftools because it seeds the completed normalization checkpoint and does not exercise normalization.
- **Verification:** bundled Python `py_compile` and `git diff --check` passed. With temporary dependencies resolved from `pyproject.toml`, focused annotation/resource/worker-state tests passed **37**; the broader workflow wrapper and recovery-matrix group passed **55**; the capacity-preflight regression group passed **9**. Full suite excluding `tests/test_report_pdf.py` passed **917 passed, 36 skipped**. The unfiltered full suite's remaining failure is the PDF renderer: Windows could not load WeasyPrint's `libgobject-2.0-0`. The first test run also found a stale test double that did not accept the database argument and a missing module-level `pytest` import; both test defects were corrected and the relevant suites passed. No CI result exists for these working-tree changes.
- **Status / next:** changes are committed and pushed on local `main` tracking `origin/main`. Continue route/worker analysis. Phase A remains PARTIAL; browser/Firebase, deployed two-tenant API/artifact isolation, and real-storage reviewer-to-signout recovery are unverified.

### 2026-10-19 concurrent VCF uploads preserve case readiness (working tree)

- **Finding:** simultaneous primary VCF uploads could each compute the case state before the other transaction committed. A valid upload could set `READY_FOR_ANALYSIS`, then a concurrent invalid upload could commit a stale `INVALID` state even though a valid VCF was persisted.
- **Correction:** recompute VCF intake status only after locking and refreshing the case row, then query persisted primary VCF statuses and update the case within the same transaction. The audit before-state is taken from the refreshed locked row.
- **Regression coverage:** added a PostgreSQL concurrency integration test that stages valid and invalid VCF artifact rows in concurrent transactions, synchronizes them before status recomputation, and asserts that both rows persist and the final case state remains `READY_FOR_ANALYSIS`.
- **Verification:** focused artifact/intake tests passed **15**; the PostgreSQL concurrency test was **skipped** because this environment has no PostgreSQL `DATABASE_URL`, so the row-lock race is not yet exercised against PostgreSQL here. Targeted Ruff checks for the new test and import order passed; `git diff --check` passed. The route-wide Ruff invocation also reported pre-existing `B008` and `BLE001` findings outside this change; those are not represented as fixed.
- **Status / next:** committed and pushed to `main` as `4f05f3b`; VCF readiness PostgreSQL integration verification initially failed in runs #392298-#392310 due to a case-row lock upgrade deadlock. This was corrected and the full exact-SHA workflow passed on follow-up `2bcd22c` in run #392315. Continue direct-object/API and recovery audit. Phase A remains PARTIAL; live Firebase/browser identity, deployed two-tenant API/artifact isolation, and real-storage reviewer-to-signout recovery remain unverified.

### 2026-10-19 versioned clinical-governance writes serialized (working tree)

- **Finding:** concurrent confirmation or secondary-finding submissions could both read the same latest version and attempt to write `MAX(version)+1`. The database uniqueness constraints would reject one request rather than persisting a complete supersession history.
- **Correction:** both versioned write routes now lock and refresh the owning analysis row before allocating a version, then re-check tenant ownership under the lock. The read-only history and non-versioned follow-up update paths are unchanged.
- **Regression coverage:** added a PostgreSQL integration test for both record types. Two concurrent production route calls must persist versions 1 and 2 with an intact supersedes chain and two audit records.
- **Verification:** `tests/test_clinical_followup_persistence.py` plus `tests/test_m12_clinical_governance.py` and the new integration module: **6 passed, 2 skipped** (the two PostgreSQL parameter cases skipped because this environment has no PostgreSQL URL). Targeted Ruff checks passed; `git diff --check` passed. This does not demonstrate PostgreSQL row-lock behavior locally.
- **Status / next:** committed and pushed to `main` as `72f30a9`; its PostgreSQL integration job failed in run #392307 along with the earlier VCF concurrency failure. The lock correction in `2bcd22c` subsequently passed PostgreSQL integration and the full workflow in run #392315. Phase A remains PARTIAL; live Firebase/browser identity, deployed two-tenant API/artifact isolation, and real-storage reviewer-to-signout recovery remain unverified.

### PostgreSQL CI follow-up — concurrent VCF case lock mode (2026-10-19)

- **Observed evidence:** PostgreSQL integration first failed on `4f05f3b` in [run #392298](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/39229846552); it failed again on `72f30a9` in [run #392307](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/39230702456) and on ledger-only `36cb73a` in [run #392310](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/39231005975). Backend and frontend jobs passed in those runs. GitHub’s public job-log endpoint returned HTTP 403, so the exact failing test name is unavailable from this session.
- **Root-cause analysis:** the new PostgreSQL concurrency test inserts child `Artifact` rows before locking the parent `Case`. PostgreSQL foreign-key checks hold KEY SHARE locks on the parent; two upload transactions then requesting `FOR UPDATE` can deadlock while upgrading their locks. This is consistent with the first failure appearing in the commit that introduced this test and with the repeated PostgreSQL-only failure. SQLAlchemy documents `key_share=True` as PostgreSQL `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, which remains a writer-serializing row lock while being compatible with KEY SHARE.
- **Correction verified:** the case readiness recomputation uses `with_for_update(key_share=True)`. This emits PostgreSQL `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, serializing competing readiness writers while remaining compatible with the artifact foreign-key KEY SHARE lock ([SQLAlchemy documentation](https://docs.sqlalchemy.org/en/20/core/selectable.html#sqlalchemy.sql.expression.GenerativeSelect.with_for_update)). The concurrent valid/invalid VCF regression passed in exact-main CI.
- **Status:** committed and pushed as `2bcd22c`. Exact-SHA [CI run #392315](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/39231528013) passed backend, PostgreSQL integration, and frontend jobs. The earlier PostgreSQL failures are resolved by this lock-mode correction. Phase A remains PARTIAL pending live/deployed gates.
