### 2026-10-19 ACMG engine status gate (local, uncommitted)

- **Finding:** `backend/app/acmg/engine.py::ACMGEngine.classify` combined every supplied criterion assessment without checking its status. Production assessment-service callers currently filter to `PROPOSED`, but the engine is also directly imported by the API and is a reusable boundary; passing `NOT_MET`, `REJECTED`, `REQUIRES_REVIEW`, or `NOT_APPLICABLE` could let a non-proposed criterion contribute to a classification.
- **Correction:** `validate_assessments` now rejects every assessment whose status is not exactly `PROPOSED` before combination. This preserves the existing human-review/criterion-evaluation distinction and fails closed for future callers.
- **Regression coverage:** `tests/test_acmg_engine.py` adds parameterized checks for `NOT_MET`, `REJECTED`, `REQUIRES_REVIEW`, and `NOT_APPLICABLE`, alongside a valid proposed assessment.
- **Verification:** bundled Python 3.12 `py_compile` passed for the changed engine and test file; `git diff --check` passed. An isolated runtime check passed five assertions (the four non-proposed states are rejected and a proposed state follows the baseline engine). Local `pytest tests/test_acmg_engine.py -q` was **NOT RUN** because the available bundled interpreter has no `pytest` module or project dependencies. The change was pushed as `e416a4e`; exact-SHA [CI run #392456](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/39245616855) completed successfully for backend, PostgreSQL integration, and frontend jobs.
- **Scientific basis / limit:** ACMG/AMP classification combines evidence criteria only after evidence is assessed; current ClinGen SVI guidance also makes criteria applicability and evidence-specific rules specification-dependent. This guard enforces the status contract but does not validate criterion assignment, a gene-specific specification, or clinical classification. See [ACMG/AMP 2015](https://www.nature.com/articles/gim201530) and [ClinGen Variant Classification Guidance](https://www.clinicalgenome.org/tools/clingen-variant-classification-guidance/).

### 2026-10-20 case-level authorization workstream

- **Starting repository state:** branch `main`, HEAD `061f3718a65494c2e3d35e81175039dae1116b03`; tracked tree was clean before this work. Existing untracked `siraloom.egg-info/` and `t Backup location…` were preserved and not edited.
- **Policy decision:** the user selected assigned-user case access with administrative bypass. New grants are scoped to a case and user and carry separate read, write, and review capabilities. Organization roles remain an upper bound; active `platform_admin`, `organization_admin`, and `lab_director` memberships bypass individual case grants within their tenant.
- **Root cause:** case APIs enforced organization membership/tenant ownership but had no per-case ACL. Thus any active member could access every case in the organization. List routes also needed a grant-aware predicate, and resource/report APIs needed read/write/review capabilities propagated through their owning case.
- **Implementation:** added `CaseAccessGrant`, migration `0048_case_access_grants` (one-time full grants for active organization members on existing cases; later membership does not imply case access), administrator grant/list/revoke endpoints with audit events, creator grant on case creation, grant-filtered case/reanalysis lists, and capability checks across case, analysis, file, evidence context, pedigree, phenotype, QC, review, report, export, and reanalysis endpoints. Added case dossier controls for administrators/directors to manage each active member's read/write/review grant. List bypass now derives the administrator role from the current membership row in SQL, preventing a stale elevated principal from retaining case enumeration after role demotion.
- **Regression evidence:** added focused permission, role-cap, cross-tenant concealment, stale-role enumeration, admin grant/revoke/audit, and legacy migration backfill tests. Updated unit-test fixtures that intentionally bypass authorization to mock the new boundary and updated access-helper mocks to accept the permission argument.

## 2026-10-20 preserved VCF records validated against the selected reference

- **Repository evidence:** work resumed on local `main` at `9cfde2fd1cfce13c9b804f03e17266cf72712fa9`, matching `origin/main`; the tracked tree was clean apart from this task's in-progress `vcf_validation.py` edit. Untracked `siraloom.egg-info/` and `t Backup location…` were preserved. Implementation and tests were committed as `bd7b93c` (`Validate preserved VCF records against selected reference`); the evidence-ledger update was committed as `b5a07ba`. `git push origin main` succeeded for both commits. `git status` shows local `main` tracking `origin/main` without divergence. A subsequent `git ls-remote` attempt failed because this session could not connect to github.com; no newer direct remote observation is claimed.
- **Workflow trace and defect:** `run_variant_analysis` classifies and durably routes GVCF reference blocks, symbolic SVs, breakends, and context-valid `*` records; only sequence-resolved variants are sent through the BCFtools normalization wrapper. Before this fix, once a reference package was selected, the worker checked source contig names but did not compare REF alleles and record spans for the preserved record types against that selected FASTA. A mixed input could therefore normalize its SNV/indel subset while retaining a structural/GVCF row incompatible with the selected reference.
- **Scientific basis:** the repository pins BCFtools 1.19. Its official versioned documentation describes `bcftools norm` for indel normalization, REF checks, and multiallelic splitting, and `bcftools view -A` for removing unseen `<NON_REF>`/`<*>` alleles at variant sites; this does not establish full GVCF reference-block or structural-variant interpretation. VCFv4.3 defines `END` as the reference span for symbolic/GVCF records and defines BND mate loci/orientation in ALT; its examples include reciprocal mates with the same bracket orientation. Consequently, the validator checks BND `SVTYPE`, explicit MATEID existence/reciprocal links/coordinates when provided, and selected-reference mate bounds, without assuming mates must use opposite brackets. Sources: [BCFtools 1.19 documentation](https://github.com/samtools/bcftools/blob/1.19/doc/bcftools.txt), [VCFv4.3 specification](https://github.com/samtools/hts-specs/blob/master/VCFv4.3.tex).
- **Correction:** after selecting a governed reference package, the worker now checks every source record's REF against the selected FASTA, validates `END` against contig length, and validates breakend remote contigs/coordinates before BCFtools transforms the eligible sequence subset. On mismatch it leaves the analysis and normalization step durably `BLOCKED` with a specific code and `REFERENCE_OR_INPUT_REVIEW`, and the audit/step lineage identifies the persisted source and routed artifacts. Original/routed records, alleles, genotype and INFO/FORMAT content remain unchanged in those preserved artifacts. Normalized output and workflow metadata include the per-source reference-validation profile alongside existing package, tool, and artifact provenance.
- **Regression evidence:** `tests/test_vcf_validation.py` covers reference agreement for a GVCF block, symbolic `<DEL>`, sequence deletion plus contextual `*`, and same-bracket reciprocal BND pair; it also covers reference mismatch in a preserved symbolic record plus missing/invalid BND linkage. The production-wrapper regression in `tests/test_unsupported_vcf_worker_routing.py` persists a mixed GVCF with a block, eligible concrete call, indel, valid `<DEL>`, and contextual `*`; it checks record counts, source SHA-256, routed artifact checksums, step/audit lineage, normalized record count, and reference profile. A symbolic-record REF mismatch is asserted to block before normalization with `VCF_REFERENCE_MISMATCH`, while retaining and linking all routed artifacts.
- **Verification:** focused command `$py -m pytest tests/test_vcf_validation.py tests/test_unsupported_vcf_worker_routing.py -q` with the temporary Python 3.12 environment: **33 passed**. Broad backend command `$py -m pytest -q --ignore=tests/test_report_pdf.py`: **1,043 passed, 40 skipped** (57.93s); one Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. `py_compile` passed for both changed implementation modules and `git diff --check` passed. The full unfiltered suite was not rerun after this change; its known platform failure is the WeasyPrint PDF test requiring unavailable Windows `libgobject-2.0-0`.
- **NOT RUN / BLOCKED locally:** the exact pinned-BCFtools executable golden module and PostgreSQL integration module were explicitly invoked and yielded **22 skipped**: no `bcftools`/Docker/Podman executable was present and `DATABASE_URL` was unset. Thus local wrapper tests use a deterministic normalization adapter and SQLite; they do not prove behavior of the actual BCFtools 1.19 executable or PostgreSQL. CI execution on the pushed exact SHA is still required for that evidence. The push succeeded, but its GitHub Actions run could not be inspected here (GitHub Actions page returned a cache miss and direct remote networking was unavailable); do not assume it passed.
- **Scope and limitation:** eligible sequence-resolved SNVs/small indels (including GVCF concrete calls only when wildcard alleles are demonstrably uncalled) continue to the reference-aware normalizer. GVCF blocks, unresolved wildcard rows, symbolic SVs, BNDs, and spanning-deletion records are reference-checked and preserved for review but remain `NOT_ANALYZED`; they do not receive SV/GVCF interpretation or sign-out. This fix does not validate assembly identity beyond the governed package/contig/REF checks, and does not validate all structural genotype semantics. Phase A remains **PARTIAL**; clinical validity and laboratory deployment readiness are not established.

### 2026-10-20 membership role-demotion case-grant revocation

- **Finding:** runtime authorization bounded case grants by current role, but membership role changes left old grant flags persisted. Later promotion could reactivate write/review permissions without a new case assignment.
- **Fix:** role changes now clear every write/review grant that the new role cannot exercise, scoped to cases in the member's organization. Read access is preserved. The revocation count and capabilities are recorded in an authenticated human audit event in the same transaction; later promotion does not restore the cleared flags. A regression covers tenant scoping and promotion behavior.
- **Verification:** focused membership/case-access/auth audit suite: **17 passed**. Full backend suite excluding the unavailable Windows-native PDF renderer: **1,006 passed, 40 skipped, 1 deprecation warning**. Skips include PostgreSQL and BCFtools cases unavailable in this environment. `git diff --check` passed.
- **Status:** this improves automated role/assignment safeguards; it does not prove deployed tenant isolation. Phase A remains PARTIAL; live identity/browser, deployed two-tenant storage/API, real-storage signout/recovery, scientific benchmark and lab deployment/restore evidence remain incomplete.

### 2026-10-20 contradictory VCF assembly declarations

- **Research basis:** the official HTS specifications document for VCF v4.5 (read 2026-10-20, https://github.com/samtools/hts-specs/blob/master/VCFv4.5.tex) says reference/contig metadata is recommended for VCF but optional, and metadata lines (when present) must be well-formed and may appear in any order. Accordingly, SIRALOOM treats recognized assembly labels as consistency evidence, never as proof or automatic build selection.
- **Finding:** ingestion used a last-recognized `##reference` assignment; a VCF with contradictory recognized assembly declarations could be accepted whenever the last one matched the user's selected build. It also ignored recognized assembly declarations carried by `##contig` tags.
- **Fix:** ingestion now recognizes bounded GRCh37/hg19 and GRCh38/hg38 tokens across reference and contig assembly metadata, and rejects a header that declares more than one of those builds. Consistent declarations remain informational; missing or unrecognized declarations still require explicit user assembly selection and later validation against the governed reference package and record REF alleles.
- **Verification:** focused ingestion, upload-capacity and reference-validation suite: **47 passed**. Full backend suite excluding the unavailable Windows-native PDF renderer: **1,011 passed, 40 skipped, 1 deprecation warning**. `git diff --check` passed. Skipped cases include external PostgreSQL and BCFtools requirements.
- **Limitations:** header declarations are self-reported and do not identify a trustworthy sequence assembly by themselves. This change does not add assembly liftover or infer assembly from coordinates. Phase A remains PARTIAL; laboratory and clinical readiness are not established.
- **Git evidence:** implementation and tests were pushed to `main` as `92057a89511bb770e74a09de54af8fe689f412ed`; local `main` and `origin/main` matched after push. Exact-SHA CI was not verified in this session. The unrelated untracked `siraloom.egg-info/` directory and backup artifact remain untouched.

### 2026-10-20 VCF fileformat placement and BOM conformance

- **Research basis:** official HTS VCF v4.2 and v4.3 specifications ([v4.2](https://github.com/samtools/hts-specs/blob/master/VCFv4.2.tex), [v4.3](https://github.com/samtools/hts-specs/blob/master/VCFv4.3.tex), read 2026-10-20) require the `##fileformat` field on the first file line; VCF v4.3 explicitly prohibits a byte-order mark.
- **Finding:** strict validation accepted `##fileformat` after another line or a leading blank line, and UTF-8-sig decoding silently removed a BOM before the validator could detect it. The general VCF record parser also silently stripped BOMs.
- **Fix:** strict VCF decoding now preserves BOMs for detection, requires `##fileformat` on physical line one, and rejects BOM-prefixed plain and gzip VCFs with typed validation codes. The general record parser also reports and rejects a BOM instead of normalizing it away. Intake documentation reflects these rules.
- **Verification:** focused VCF validation/intake/worker/tool suite: **58 passed, 5 skipped** (BCFtools-dependent checks). Full backend suite excluding Windows-native PDF renderer: **1,017 passed, 40 skipped, 1 deprecation warning**. `git diff --check` passed. PostgreSQL/BCFtools services are unavailable locally; exact-SHA CI remains unverified.
- **Status / limitation:** this enforces VCF format requirements for the currently accepted v4.2/v4.3 input scope. It does not add support for VCFv4.4/v4.5 or establish scientific truth-set concordance. Phase A remains PARTIAL.
- **Git evidence:** implementation and tests are pushed to `main` as `76eb5fabb9a32098a190b206364dfac368d8ba00`; local `main` and `origin/main` match. Exact-SHA CI remains unverified. The unrelated untracked `siraloom.egg-info/` and backup artifact remain untouched.

### 2026-10-21 gnomAD GraphQL provider correction

- **Research basis:** on 2026-10-21 I inspected the current official [gnomAD GraphQL API README](https://github.com/broadinstitute/gnomad-browser/blob/main/graphql-api/README.md) and [official variant schema](https://github.com/broadinstitute/gnomad-browser/blob/main/graphql-api/src/graphql/types/variant.graphql). The official schema places `joint`, `exome`, and `genome` under `VariantDetails`, with allele counts and population arrays nested below them. It defines `homozygote_count`; `ac_hom` is deprecated. The README says the API schema is under development, so a live compatibility qualification remains necessary.
- **Defect and correction:** the previous request selected top-level `ac`, `an`, and `populations`, which the current schema does not declare; successful null lookup also returned an empty list, leaving no durable query/response evidence. The adapter now requests joint and separate exome/genome values, persists `GLOBAL`/`MID` joint observations and distinct cohort observations without summing cohorts, writes explicit `NOT_AVAILABLE` ancestry records and provenance, and persists successful exact no-match outcomes as null-valued `NOT_FOUND` records. Other GraphQL errors and malformed responses fail explicitly. Local tabix no-match gets a response hash and query metadata. ACMG criteria only consume cohort-specific codes when a rule explicitly configures them. Variant export JSON schema advanced to 1.1.0 and includes separate cohort AF fields.
- **Verification:** focused command `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest tests/test_gnomad.py tests/test_gnomad_provenance.py tests/test_evidence_engine.py tests/test_criterion_evaluators.py tests/test_population_provider_retry.py tests/test_actual_population_stage_redelivery.py tests/test_variant_manifest_consumers.py tests/test_population_route_tenant_guards.py -q`: **48 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Final broad backend command `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -q --ignore=tests/test_report_pdf.py`: **1,116 passed, 42 skipped**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Local PDF rendering remains excluded due to missing Windows `libgobject-2.0-0`. `git diff --check` passed. Ruff reported pre-existing style/import findings in touched modules; no Ruff-clean claim is made.
- **Limitations:** tests use controlled GraphQL fixtures and SQLite, not the live gnomAD endpoint or laboratory VCF. The API is mutable and not pinned to a schema release. Scientific benchmarks, clinical validity, and laboratory qualification are not established. Phase A and scientific validation remain **PARTIAL**; live identity, deployed tenant/storage isolation, real-storage reviewer-to-signout recovery, truth-set benchmarking, and lab deployment/restore remain unverified.

### 2026-10-21 gnomAD missing-allele-number semantics follow-up

- **Research basis:** the official [gnomAD v3.1.1 release notes](https://gnomad.broadinstitute.org/news/2021-03-gnomad-v3-1-1/) describe absent-subset frequency data previously represented as `AC=0`, `AN=null`, and homozygote count 0; the release changed the complete struct to missing. The official [gnomAD frequency display notice](https://gnomad.broadinstitute.org/news/2023-08-modify-population-frequency-table/) says an `AN` of zero must not be displayed as frequency zero. The current [GraphQL API documentation](https://github.com/broadinstitute/gnomad-browser/blob/main/graphql-api/README.md) warns that its schema can change.
- **Finding and correction:** the GraphQL response path initially treated mismatched nullness between allele count and allele number as malformed, and considered a population available when only homozygote data existed. The local tabix path also accepted a source `AF` even when its `AN` was missing or zero. Both paths now preserve partial/missing counts, expose frequency evidence only when valid AC and positive AN are present, and suppress source or derived AF when AN is unavailable/nonpositive. Null dataset sections produce explicit unavailable observations; malformed non-null GraphQL objects still fail. Invalid GraphQL types, negative counts, AC greater than AN, malformed population arrays, and duplicate population IDs still fail explicitly.
- **Regression evidence:** GraphQL fixtures with `AC=0, AN=null` for an ancestry and `AC=0, AN=0` for a cohort remain `NOT_AVAILABLE` with null AF; a partial `AC=0` is preserved without becoming evidence of zero frequency. Local-tabix tests cover source AF alongside missing, zero, and negative AN; a GraphQL fixture covers null dataset sections. Additional tests reject duplicate population IDs case-insensitively and missing count fields in a non-null section.
- **Executed verification:** `.venv\\Scripts\\python.exe -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp C:\\Windows\\Temp\\siraloom-pytest -q tests\\test_gnomad.py tests\\test_gnomad_provenance.py tests\\test_population_provider_policy.py tests\\test_population_provider_retry.py tests\\test_secondary_population_provider.py tests\\test_case_access_control.py tests\\test_case_access_migration.py` — **37 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. `git diff --check` passed after normalizing the two Python files to LF. The focused test was run against controlled GraphQL fixtures; no live gnomAD request or independent truth-set benchmark was performed.
- **Authorization evidence:** the assigned-user policy already present in `main` was separately verified by its case-access and migration tests in that same run: assigned capabilities remain bounded by organization roles, unassigned users are denied, tenant admins bypass only within their organization, and the migration snapshots existing active members while excluding suspended memberships.
- **Linux verification:** isolated environment installed from the repository's documented development extra (`python -m pip install -e '.[dev]'`). Focused command `/tmp/siraloom-linux-test-env/bin/python -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp /tmp/siraloom-pytest-final -q tests/test_gnomad.py tests/test_gnomad_provenance.py` — **19 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Adjacent command `/tmp/siraloom-linux-test-env/bin/python -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp /tmp/siraloom-pytest-broader-final -q tests/test_population_provider_policy.py tests/test_population_provider_retry.py tests/test_secondary_population_provider.py tests/test_actual_population_stage_redelivery.py tests/test_evidence_engine.py tests/test_criterion_evaluators.py` — **32 passed**, same warning. Staged `git diff --check` passed. These are deterministic fixtures, not a live gnomAD/API qualification or independent scientific benchmark.
- **Status / next:** the AC/AN missing-value correction is **VERIFIED** against the stated controlled GraphQL and local-tabix regression cases on Linux. It is not yet committed or pushed; local `main` and remote `main` were both `a85d5d496616dae57c75fff5ad0ed22f3419a3db` before this commit. GitHub Actions logs remain unavailable because the GitHub connection is not active. Phase A and overall scientific validation remain **PARTIAL**; live external resources, deployed two-tenant checks, real-storage sign-out recovery, truth-set validation, and laboratory deployment/restore remain unverified.

### 2026-10-22 worker authorization and shared-resource reanalysis scan follow-up

- **Repository state:** Linux checkout `/home/usama/SIRALOOM`, branch `main`, starting commit `90f5f23271731ece40997ccd711ffe0a315b956f`, remote `origin/main` observed at the same SHA before this work. - **Findings:** a tenant candidate-resource registration could enqueue the global active-resource scanner before qualification/activation, allowing that tenant action to create candidates and notifications in other laboratories. The scan now runs after activation and takes the activating lab's organization ID; global shared-resource activation remains a platform-admin operation. Review then found that `organization_id` was reused as the per-tenant loop variable in the global scanner, so later shared resources could be scanned only for the last tenant encountered. The daily Beat scan remains the recovery route. A regression injects a Kombu `OperationalError` and verifies that activation and its audit event remain durable, the result signals failed dispatch, and secret-like exception text is not logged.

- **Repository state:** `/home/usama/SIRALOOM`, branch `main`, base `HEAD` `b736f773a054eed4c5e6111a800874c8e4aeb7e4`; `origin/main` matched. Existing uncommitted HPO changes were preserved and reviewed. Untracked `siraloom.egg-info/` was excluded from the changes.
- **Research basis:** HPO publishes dated releases and an OBO artifact; the official 2026-09-01 release asset records SHA-256 `8cf51771f19a1168871ef3ded0da86c51e4c9630068da726ece7eb61406a2418` ([release](https://github.com/obophenotype/human-phenotype-ontology/releases/tag/v2026-09-01)). HPO documentation describes its ontology and disease phenotype annotations; OBO 1.2 allows trailing modifiers on relationship values ([HPO annotation format](https://github.com/obophenotype/human-phenotype-ontology/blob/master/docs/annotations/phenotype_hpoa.md), [OBO 1.2 format](https://owlcollab.github.io/oboformat/doc/GO.format.obo-1_2.html)). These sources establish file and syntax semantics; they do not validate SIRALOOM's ranking algorithm or clinical use.
- **Implementation:** added `HPOOboProvider`, a local-only provider that requires a matching SHA-256 and declared release, validates terms and `is_a` graph integrity, resolves current/alternate IDs and only explicit unambiguous obsolete replacements, and reports unknown, invalid and ambiguous terms without guessing. Registered it through the existing provider registry and mapped HPO resources to the existing phenotype capability. The evidence workflow resolves the organization/case-pinned HPO resource, checks its checksum and release against its qualified execution contract, records request/response hashes and resource qualification provenance, and uses only ontology-derived `is_a` ancestors. Missing or invalid ontology data produces `REQUIRES_REVIEW`; unpinned exact overlap is diagnostic metadata and is not emitted as a ranking score. Negative-only and directly contradictory present/absent patient terms also produce `REQUIRES_REVIEW` without a similarity score. Malformed or duplicate `is_obsolete` declarations are rejected. Worker replay tests cover HPO success and checksum failure, including evidence-row identity across redelivery.
- **Verification:** `PYTHONPATH=. /tmp/siraloom-hpo-env/bin/python -m pytest -q tests/test_hpo_obo_provider.py tests/test_m8_phenotype_evidence.py tests/test_analysis_resource_profiles.py tests/test_resource_registry.py tests/test_actual_population_stage_redelivery.py tests/test_evidence_engine.py tests/test_evidence_provenance.py tests/test_actual_annotation_stage_redelivery.py tests/test_evidence_persisted_count.py tests/test_gnomad.py tests/test_gnomad_provenance.py` — **86 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. The suite uses deterministic fixtures/SQLite for application execution; it is not a laboratory deployment or independent benchmark. Against the separately downloaded official HPO artifact, the provider verified the expected digest and release and parsed 20,482 terms, 3,445 aliases, and 24 ambiguous aliases; spot checks resolved `HP:0001250` as Seizure, mapped obsolete `HP:0001425` to `HP:0000005`, and held `HP:0200135` as ambiguous. `ruff check` passed for the new adapter, its tests, and the worker replay test; `py_compile`, `pip check`, and `git diff --check` passed. A Ruff run over the large pre-existing workflow/evidence modules reported existing findings elsewhere; no clean claim is made for those modules.

### 2026-10-23 case-export recovery after a failed worker task

- **Repository state:** `/home/usama/SIRALOOM`, branch `main`, base `HEAD` `d8df9c11e1b7d9da6499f3b1ea98f39400618ce2`; `origin/main` matched before this change. Existing untracked `siraloom.egg-info/` is preserved.
- **Finding:** the case-export worker can fail while its database is unavailable, leaving `CaseExport.status == RUNNING` even though the current Celery task is terminal. Existing orphan recovery handled queued exports without a durable task pointer, but not this running/failed-task state.
- **Correction:** periodic recovery now inspects only exports whose persisted current task maps to a PUBLISHED dispatch. It retries only definitive Celery `FAILURE` or `REVOKED` states, supersedes the old dispatch, creates a new generation, transitions the export to QUEUED, and records recovery audit in the same transaction. PENDING, STARTED, RETRY, and unknown states are left unchanged. Four dispatch generations are allowed; after a definitive failure at the limit, the dispatch and export become terminally FAILED with an atomic audit event.
- **Research basis:** Celery's official [result API documentation](https://docs.celeryq.dev/en/stable/reference/celery.result.html) defines FAILURE/REVOKED as terminal failure states and RETRY as a retry condition. Its [getting-started task-state documentation](https://docs.celeryq.dev/en/stable/getting-started/next-steps.html) explains that PENDING is also the default state for an unknown task ID, so recovery must not treat it as proof of task loss.
- **Regression coverage:** added tests for nonterminal/unknown states, exactly-once recovery and audit under repeated recovery passes, and terminal retry exhaustion. The fixtures use SQLite and fake Celery result states; they validate the database state machine and audit persistence, not production broker/result-backend semantics or PostgreSQL locking races.
- **Verification:** `PYTHONPATH=. DATABASE_URL=sqlite:// REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0 /tmp/siraloom-hpo-env/bin/python -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp /tmp/siraloom-case-export-recovery-final2 -q tests/test_case_export_dispatch_recovery.py tests/test_case_export_dispatch.py tests/test_case_export_error_redaction.py tests/test_case_export_artifact_compensation.py tests/test_case_export.py tests/test_celery_dispatch_fence_audit.py tests/test_worker_dispatch_generation_fence.py tests/test_queue_dispatch_recovery.py` — **57 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Ruff on the new test, `py_compile` for changed Python files, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Limitations/status:** **VERIFIED** for deterministic local recovery behavior with the above fixtures. Deployed Celery result-backend expiry, Postgres concurrent recovery, and real storage recovery were not exercised. If task results expire before the periodic recovery sees FAILURE, the export may remain RUNNING; this remains an operational limitation for a subsequent durable task-state audit. This change does not complete the global crash/replay matrix or Phase A. No clinical validation or laboratory qualification is claimed.
- **Commit and release gates:** implementation commit `4691797c3e38cb818345926078f1959bda654f38` is on `origin/main`. Exact-SHA GitHub Actions run [39932178036](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/39932178036) completed successfully: backend tests/container build, frontend tests/build/container validation, and PostgreSQL/real-Celery worker-loss integration all passed. GitHub Production deployment `7274847222` reports success at [siraloom-o2s151c0n-sira14.vercel.app](https://siraloom-o2s151c0n-sira14.vercel.app). That deployment record does not exercise Celery, PostgreSQL, Redis, or the case-export recovery path at runtime.

### 2026-10-23 block adoption of resources in disallowed lifecycle states

- **Repository state:** `/home/usama/SIRALOOM`, branch `main`, base `HEAD` `e3d9a82a482d642861e9bc13a266e260099ade73`; remote `main` matched before this change. The untracked `siraloom.egg-info/` remains preserved.
- **Finding:** resource approval request and decision validated a successful qualification but did not re-check the resource's current lifecycle status. A previously qualified resource could become REJECTED or QUARANTINED after qualification and still create a pending request or be adopted, despite runtime refusing those states.
- **Correction:** approval request and decision now require the resource to be ACTIVE, QUALIFIED, or SUPERSEDED, and lock the resource/qualification/approval rows while checking the lifecycle and applying the transition. Candidate, rejected and quarantined resources cannot be submitted; a resource quarantined after request cannot be approved. Historical superseded resources remain eligible for explicit organizational approval and reproducible use.
- **Regression coverage:** added status-parametrized request tests and a request-then-quarantine decision test, asserting no approval/binding is created for disallowed states. Existing adoption, fallback, registry and profile-binding tests remain in the focused run.
- **Verification:** `PYTHONPATH=. Full Ruff on the changed domain module and test, `py_compile`, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Limitations/status:** **VERIFIED** for lifecycle rejection behavior using deterministic SQLite fixtures. ### 2026-10-23 repair lifecycle-guard test fixtures after CI

- **Finding:** CI run [39934765091](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/39934765091) for `b5bc8c2a037279cf165609e583c9e9dbf3494fa7` failed in two resource-adoption tests. The API fixture manually paired a `CANDIDATE` resource with a successful `QUALIFIED` row, an impossible lifecycle state; the PostgreSQL concurrency test synchronized at ORM flush, after the new resource row lock, forcing the lock holder to wait for a second caller that could not reach flush.
- **Corrections:** the API test now sets its resource to `QUALIFIED` before inserting the qualified record. The PostgreSQL test synchronizes both caller threads before they enter the service; it continues to test two simultaneous idempotent requests while respecting the newly required resource lock.

### 2026-10-23 require HPO ontology identity and supported OBO format

- **Audit finding:** the pinned HPO adapter verified the SHA-256 and release date and parsed `is_a` terms, but it did not validate the OBO header's ontology identity or format version. A separately qualified/mislabeled OBO file containing HP-shaped identifiers, or a future syntax revision, could be accepted and interpreted by this parser as HPO.
- **Research basis:** the official HPO `v2026-09-01` `hp.obo` artifact declares `format-version: 1.2`, `data-version: hp/releases/2026-09-01`, and `ontology: hp.obo` ([official release artifact](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/obophenotype/human-phenotype-ontology/v2026-09-01/hp.obo)); the OBO parser already handles OBO 1.2 relationship modifiers. These header checks establish resource identity/syntax compatibility; they do not validate phenotype matching or clinical use.
- **Correction:** the adapter now requires exactly one `format-version: 1.2`, exactly one `ontology: hp.obo`, and rejects repeated identity/release header fields before accepting a resource. The real workflow's HPO replay fixtures now declare the official ontology identity. Phenotype scoring behavior was not changed.

### 2026-10-23 reject invalid local gnomAD count annotations

- **Finding:** GraphQL count validation rejects negative/non-integer values and `AC > AN`, but the local-tabix path parsed negative and malformed integer fields without rejecting them. The prior regression explicitly allowed `AN=-1` to be persisted as an unavailable observation. This could retain invalid provider data alongside otherwise valid evidence.
- **Research basis:** the official VCF v4.5 specification defines reserved `AC` as an integer allele count per ALT and `AN` as the total count of alleles in called genotypes ([HTS specifications](https://samtools.github.io/hts-specs/VCFv4.5.pdf), accessed 2026-10-23). The gnomAD v4.1 field descriptions identify `AC` as alternate allele count ([gnomAD discussion quoting release VCF headers](https://discuss.gnomad.broadinstitute.org/t/clarification-on-gnomad-v4-1-data-in-gcp/777/2), accessed 2026-10-23). The implementation therefore treats negative/non-integer counts and `AC > AN` as invalid source data, while retaining explicit missing values (`.`) as unavailable.
- **Correction:** the local provider now validates selected AC, AN and homozygote-count fields as non-negative integers, rejects count vectors missing the requested ALT value, and rejects AC exceeding AN. `AC=0, AN=.` remains a valid partial observation with a null AF; AN zero remains unavailable. Removed the obsolete unchecked scalar parser.
- **Regression coverage:** local-tabix fixtures now reject negative AC, negative AN, `AC > AN`, negative homozygote counts, and malformed AC. Existing cases continue to cover `AC=0` with missing/zero AN, AF suppression when AC is missing, and valid zero frequency when AC is zero with positive AN.
- **Verification:** `DATABASE_URL=sqlite:// REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0 /tmp/siraloom-linux-verify-env/bin/python -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp /tmp/siraloom-gnomad-invalid-counts-final -q tests/test_gnomad.py tests/test_gnomad_provenance.py tests/test_population_provider_retry.py tests/test_actual_population_stage_redelivery.py` — **34 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. A full Ruff check reports pre-existing lint findings in these files (import ordering/unused import and legacy modernization rules); no clean full-file lint claim is made.

### 2026-10-23 reject invalid local gnomAD AF values

- **Finding:** the local provider's permissive float conversion accepted negative, greater-than-one, NaN, infinite, and malformed AF values. The VCF reserved INFO definition describes AF as a frequency for each alternate allele, estimated from primary data; these values cannot represent a valid frequency.
- **Research basis:** official VCF v4.5 reserved INFO definitions specify AF as a `Float` allele frequency per ALT and AC/AN as integer counts ([HTS specifications](https://samtools.github.io/hts-specs/VCFv4.5.pdf), accessed 2026-10-23). Missing values are represented with `.`; invalid numeric values should not be collapsed into missing evidence.
- **Correction:** local-tabix AF parsing now preserves explicit missing values but rejects malformed, non-finite, and out-of-range values, and rejects an AF vector without a value for the selected ALT. It does not recalculate or claim AF from independent raw data.
- **Regression coverage:** tests cover negative, >1, NaN, infinite, and non-numeric source AFs while existing fixtures cover valid endpoints, valid zero frequency, and unavailable AF when AC/AN is missing.
- **Verification:** `DATABASE_URL=sqlite:// REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0 /tmp/siraloom-linux-verify-env/bin/python -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp /tmp/siraloom-gnomad-invalid-af-final -q tests/test_gnomad.py tests/test_gnomad_provenance.py tests/test_population_provider_retry.py tests/test_actual_population_stage_redelivery.py` — **39 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Adjacent population/evidence suite — **34 passed**, same warning. Targeted Ruff `--select FURB188`, Python compilation, and `git diff --check` passed. No live local dataset or truth-set benchmark was available.
- **Limitations/status:** implementation is a source-field sanity check, not qualification of a gnomAD release or independent population-frequency validation. Phase A and scientific validation remain **PARTIAL**.

### 2026-10-23 suppress population AF when allele number is unavailable

- **Finding:** GeneBe's documented variant response exposes `frequency_reference_population`, `gnomad_exomes_af`, and `gnomad_genomes_af`, but does not expose matching AN values. SIRALOOM normalized and persisted these AFs and built population evidence with `allele_number=None`. Existing report/API paths also returned historically stored AF values without checking AN.
- **Research basis:** GeneBe's official response-field reference documents these AF and AC fields without an AN field ([GeneBe variant description fields](https://docs.genebe.net/docs/api/variant-description-fields/)); the official VCF specification defines AN as allele number and AC/AF as allele count/frequency semantics ([HTS specifications](https://samtools.github.io/hts-specs/VCFv4.5.pdf), accessed 2026-10-23). Provider frequency output without a denominator does not satisfy SIRALOOM's policy for a qualified/displayable AF.
- **Correction:** GeneBe normalization now preserves AC but sets unsupported AF fields to null with `DENOMINATOR_UNAVAILABLE` status. The original provider payload remains stored for provenance, while variant and reviewer API payloads suppress its denominator-free raw AF fields. Population evidence requires positive AN for AF; report, review, population-query, variant, and case-export surfaces now suppress AF unless AN is positive and AF is finite/in range. ACMG frequency evaluators already require AF and AN, and their behavior was left intact.
- **Regression coverage:** tests cover normalized/displayed GeneBe AF suppression while retaining raw provenance and AC; evidence with missing/zero AN; legacy stored AF suppression in generated variant reports; valid AF with positive AN remains visible.

### 2026-10-23 fence terminal resource-staging task results

- **Finding:** `run_resource_staging_task` returned `STAGED` before checking the durable dispatch identity. A stale task ID could therefore receive a terminal result for a staging row it no longer owned. The dispatch matcher also rejected a legitimate same-task redelivery after the staging state and dispatch had both durably completed.
- **Correction:** the task now validates dispatch identity before returning any existing `STAGED` result. Matching `COMPLETED` dispatches are accepted only when the staging row is also `STAGED`; stale task IDs and inconsistent terminal state are rejected. No resource acquisition, file-write or approval behavior changed.
- **Regression coverage:** added execution-level cases for stale task IDs against published and completed dispatches, and idempotent same-task replay after completion; helper coverage rejects completed dispatches with a nonterminal staging row.
- **Verification:** focused worker/resource command with SQLite and Redis URL configuration covering dispatch fences, resource staging API/orchestration, staging service and queue recovery — **92 passed**. Full non-integration backend command with CI feature flags — **1,214 passed, 17 skipped, 27 deselected**. Ruff on the changed test, Python compilation of both changed Python files, and `git diff --check` passed. One existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning was emitted by pytest.
- **Limitations/status:** verified in automated SQLite/controlled worker fixtures; no live laboratory resource storage or deployed broker was exercised. Phase A remains **PARTIAL**.

### 2026-10-23 preserve child-scoped lineage for reused reanalysis evidence

- **Finding/reproduction:** incremental reanalysis cloned `PopulationObservation` rows with new IDs, but copied `Evidence.observation_ids` unchanged. The evidence fingerprint was also copied from the parent despite fingerprint identity including `analysis_id`. Reanalysis beginning at `acmg_assessment` or later therefore retained parent-row references in the child. Cloned ACMG proposals could likewise keep parent evidence IDs, and source-level ClinGen assertions were not recreated for reused evidence. The new regression initially failed on both incremental reuse cases.
- **Correction:** `_copy_rows` now maps parent population-observation IDs to child IDs; remaps copied evidence observation links; recomputes evidence fingerprints for the child analysis and mapped observations; reconstructs source assertions against the copied evidence; and remaps ACMG proposal evidence IDs. It fails closed if reused evidence IDs are malformed or an ACMG proposal references evidence outside its parent analysis. Parent rows are not modified.
- **Regression coverage:** reanalysis from population, evidence, ACMG, and reportability boundaries checks only upstream outputs are copied; child evidence points to child observations and has a child-scoped fingerprint; source assertions point to child evidence; later ACMG proposals point to child evidence. A malformed cross-analysis ACMG evidence reference is rejected and the test rolls back the transaction without persisted partial child evidence.
- **Verification:** initial regression reproduced **2 failures** on the previous implementation. Final focused reuse test: **5 passed**. Adjacent reanalysis, evidence, ACMG binding/specification and real workflow redelivery tests: **71 passed** before the final fail-closed case was added. Full non-integration backend command with CI feature flags: **1,216 passed, 17 skipped, 27 deselected**. `ruff check tests/test_reanalysis_reuse_provenance.py`, targeted `ruff check --select FURB188 backend/app/domain/reanalysis.py`, Python compilation, and `git diff --check` passed. One existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning was emitted.
- **Limitations/status:** verified against SQLite persistence and controlled workflow fixtures; no live laboratory parent/child analyses or PostgreSQL deployment were available. This verifies lineage behavior for tested incremental reuse boundaries, not scientific validity of the underlying evidence. Phase A remains **PARTIAL**.

### 2026-10-23 recover case exports after Celery result expiry

- **Finding:** case-export recovery treated only Celery `FAILURE` and `REVOKED` as evidence of worker loss. Celery task results expire according to backend configuration, so an export can remain durably `RUNNING` after its result disappears. PostgreSQL's session-level advisory lock already fences the export for the worker's full lifetime and is released when its database session ends.
- **Research basis:** the official [PostgreSQL advisory-lock documentation](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/explicit-locking.html) states that session locks persist until unlock or session end; official [Celery result configuration](https://docs.celeryq.dev/en/stable/userguide/configuration.html#result-expires) documents result expiration. These make the task result unsuitable as the sole liveness record.
- **Correction:** factor the case-export lock-key calculation into a shared helper. On PostgreSQL, recovery now tries to acquire the same advisory lock before changing a `RUNNING` export. A held lock means an execution is still active and is left untouched. An acquired lock means no worker owns the export; recovery can atomically supersede the old dispatch and persist a replacement generation while holding the lock, then releases it before publishing. A broker `SUCCESS` with a nonterminal database export is left for reconciliation. Non-PostgreSQL backends retain the conservative terminal-result-only rule. Lock-query errors invalidate/close the dedicated connection and propagate.
- **Regression coverage:** unit tests cover expired/unknown Celery states (`PENDING`, `STARTED`, `RETRY`, `UNKNOWN`) with a free lock, active lock protection, success-state protection, terminal retry exhaustion, and lock-query failure cleanup. Added a PostgreSQL integration test that simulates a missing Celery result, checks durable replacement/audit state, and proves the recovery lock is released before republishing.
- **Verification:** final focused export/recovery command, including the new PostgreSQL integration module: **27 passed, 2 skipped**; the two PostgreSQL integration checks skipped because this Linux environment has no configured `DATABASE_URL`. `ruff check tests/test_case_export_dispatch_recovery.py`, `ruff check --select F401 tests/integration/test_worker_execution_locks_postgres.py`, Python compilation of all changed Python files, and `git diff --check` passed. The full non-integration backend suite passed **1,224 passed, 17 skipped, 27 deselected** before the final lock-query cleanup guard; the final guard and its injected-failure regression were then covered by the focused run. One existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning appeared.
- **Limitations/status:** PostgreSQL integration behavior was not executed in the local Linux environment because it has no `DATABASE_URL`; the exact-SHA CI PostgreSQL integration job did execute it successfully. Deployed broker/result-backend expiry and laboratory storage were not exercised. This addresses the recorded stuck-export case but does not complete the global crash/replay matrix or Phase A.
- **Commit and release gates:** commit `50b2475897a7820a4a4b6326aa199f3ea340851e` is present on local and remote `main`. Exact-SHA GitHub Actions run [39967876009](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/39967876009) passed backend (**1,241 passed, 29 skipped**), PostgreSQL/Celery integration (**28 passed**), and frontend build/behavior/container/Compose jobs. GitHub Production deployment `7280444574` reports success at [siraloom-bxpr6xkvi-sira14.vercel.app](https://siraloom-bxpr6xkvi-sira14.vercel.app). HTTP inspection redirects to Vercel SSO, so authenticated runtime behavior is not verified.

### 2026-10-23 remove unvalidated HPO priority weights

- **Audit finding:** ontology-aware patient/disease HPO overlap was converted into a fixed `priority_adjustment` of 5, 15, or 25 points using hard-coded thresholds. No validated prioritization model, calibration evidence, or laboratory policy justified those weights. The value was emitted in persisted gene-disease evidence, even though it was not used by an executable validated clinical-ranking model.
- **Scientific basis:** HPO's official annotation documentation describes disease-phenotype frequency and negative annotations as relevant to differential diagnosis and explains that `phenotype.hpoa` carries source, evidence, frequency, onset, aspect and biocuration semantics. SIRALOOM currently uses only a subset of ontology ancestry and does not execute a governed HPOA disease-association provider; therefore its overlap fraction is not a validated disease-priority score. Sources: [HPO annotation introduction](https://obophenotype.github.io/human-phenotype-ontology/annotations/introduction/), [phenotype.hpoa format](https://github.com/obophenotype/human-phenotype-ontology/blob/master/docs/annotations/phenotype_hpoa.md), [HPO frequency semantics](https://obophenotype.github.io/human-phenotype-ontology/annotations/frequency/). This change does not implement or validate a clinical prioritization algorithm.
- **Correction:** removed the fixed-point `priority_adjustment` function and its evidence output. Kept the existing overlap fraction for transparent review and added explicit `score_semantics` and `clinical_prioritization_validated: false` metadata. No ACMG criterion, classification, reportability decision, or authorization logic consumes this score.

### 2026-10-23 connect governed HPO disease-phenotype assertions

- **Gap and execution path:** the existing worker resolved a pinned local HPO OBO ontology for term validation, but disease phenotype associations were accepted only from mutable case-context fields; there was no registered, version-pinned disease-to-phenotype provider. The evidence worker now resolves the optional `DISEASE_PHENOTYPE_ASSOCIATION` capability through its existing profile/resource plan and registry, then queries only exact disease identifiers already present in gene-disease context. No identifier crosswalk or disease inference is performed.
- **Implementation:** added a local HPOA 12-column TSV/gzip provider requiring the artifact SHA-256, HPOA release, matching HPO release, and local-only execution contract. Registered HPOA as a separate disease-phenotype capability/resource type; made it optional in the standard analysis profile and evidence-stage contract; and permitted only the immutable profile selection at runtime. Laboratory execution additionally requires an organization-owned resource and a recorded license permission. No public fetch, global fallback, or substitution is allowed. Persisted HPOA assertions are neutral `PHENOTYPE` evidence carrying exact disease ID, gene, reference, source evidence code, raw frequency, `NOT` qualifier, onset, modifiers, aspect, biocuration, resource qualification, input fingerprint and response digest. These records do not produce an ACMG criterion or a phenotype-priority score.
- **Scientific source basis:** official HPO [annotation format](https://github.com/obophenotype/human-phenotype-ontology/blob/master/docs/annotations/phenotype_hpoa.md), [annotation introduction](https://obophenotype.github.io/human-phenotype-ontology/annotations/introduction/), and [frequency semantics](https://obophenotype.github.io/human-phenotype-ontology/annotations/frequency/). HPOA's source-defined presence/absence and frequency semantics are preserved; this integration does not validate those assertions or establish diagnostic performance. HPO notes licensing restrictions for OMIM-derived annotations; no HPOA data file is included in the repository, and laboratory configuration must record applicable permission. A nonempty license declaration is an operational gate, not legal review or proof of license entitlement.
- **Regression evidence:** provider tests cover plain/gzip input, exact identifier matching, all retained assertion fields, `NOT` and `0/N`, malformed records, checksum/release mismatches, and rejection of remote execution. A production-wrapper SQLite worker-loss test verifies persisted HPOA evidence and query provenance, no duplicate resource execution after replay, and fail-closed behavior when a registered resource lacks the laboratory license declaration; optional evidence unavailability does not falsely fail unrelated work. Gene scoping is tested so another gene's disease annotations are not attached to a variant.

### Support both official HPO 2026-09-01 OBO release artifacts

- **Finding:** the local ontology adapter accepted the official `hp.obo` release artifact but rejected the official `hp-base.obo` artifact because its OBO identity is `hp/hp-base`. Both are distributed in the same signed HPO release with distinct asset checksums. A lab registering the official base ontology could therefore pass qualification yet fail at adapter load.
- **Implementation:** the HPO OBO parser now accepts only the two official identities `hp.obo` and `hp/hp-base`, while continuing to require OBO 1.2, an exact release date, a matching SHA-256, complete `is_a` parent references, and an acyclic graph. Added a regression fixture for the `hp-base` identity/release format. No matching or clinical interpretation semantics changed.
- **Scientific source:** official HPO release `v2026-09-01` publishes `hp.obo` (SHA-256 `93dace952fcb3ec4728818857f6ba76bc2d5312f4d83266519b8694b8e798f22`) and `hp-base.obo` (SHA-256 `8cf51771f19a1168871ef3ded0da86c51e4c9630068da726ece7eb61406a2418`); the release asset manifests and artifact headers were inspected and hashes independently checked after download. These checks establish parser compatibility with those artifacts, not biological validity of downstream phenotype matching.

### 2026-10-24 retire unvalidated inheritance compatibility scores

- **Audit finding:** `backend/app/inheritance.py::assess_model` converted a few genotype/affected-status combinations into “CONSISTENT” or “CONTRADICTED” and an arbitrary 0–100 score. It did not model disease mechanism, penetrance, validated pedigree transmissions, phase, parental confirmation, or disease-specific rules. The API persisted and returned these claims; review and report projections exposed score and status. In particular, an affected heterozygous proband alone could be called contradictory for a recessive model, and one unaffected carrier could contradict a dominant model despite age-dependent/reduced penetrance.
- **Scientific basis:** ACMG/AMP 2015 cautions that co-segregation is evidence for linkage and must be interpreted carefully; unaffected carriers require penetrance/age consideration and phenotype evaluation, while de novo assessment requires relevant family and confirmation context. ClinGen specifications vary by disease/gene and apply criterion-specific evidence/counting rules. Sources: [Richards et al. 2015](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/pmid/25741868/), [ClinGen CSpec GN102](https://cspec.clinicalgenome.org/cspec/ui/svi/doc/GN102), [ClinGen CSpec GN141](https://cspec.clinicalgenome.org/cspec/ui/svi/doc/GN141). This patch does not implement PP1/BS4/PS2/PM6 or an inheritance compatibility algorithm.
- **Correction:** replaced the heuristic with neutral data-sufficiency and `REQUIRES_REVIEW` outcomes; no score, auto-consistency, auto-contradiction, or de novo claim is produced. Newly stored evidence is explicitly observations-only. Made the legacy score nullable and added migration `0051_retire_unvalidated_inheritance_scores`: existing heuristic values are cleared, `CONSISTENT`/`CONTRADICTED` assessments transition to `REQUIRES_REVIEW`, and prior rationale is replaced while assessment identity, observation fingerprint, reviewer, and timestamps remain. Review and report APIs suppress old scores and map legacy M10 findings to review-required; raw audit/history remains retained. The review screen no longer displays scores and now explains that no compatibility model is calculated.
- **Regression tests:** tests cover dominant carrier/reduced-penetrance ambiguity, recessive single-heterozygote ambiguity, insufficient/missing data, unconfirmed de novo, actual API persistence with no score, and migration preservation/neutralization of historic assessments. Existing report/review/reanalysis tests exercise downstream projections and copied lineage.
- **Verification:** focused command `PYTHONPATH=. DATABASE_URL=sqlite:// REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0 /tmp/siraloom-current-venv/bin/python -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp /tmp/siraloom-inheritance-retirement-final -q --tb=short tests/test_m10_pedigree_inheritance.py tests/test_m11_inheritance_migration.py tests/test_m11_migrations.py tests/test_review_queue.py tests/test_m9_clinical_review_context.py tests/test_reporting_contract.py tests/test_report_finalization.py tests/test_reanalysis_reuse_provenance.py` — **39 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Full non-integration backend suite `PYTHONPATH=. DATABASE_URL=sqlite:////tmp/siraloom-inheritance-full.db REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0 GENEBE_ENABLED=false GNOMAD_ENABLED=false FIREBASE_AUTH_REQUIRED=false /tmp/siraloom-current-venv/bin/python -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp /tmp/siraloom-inheritance-full-run -q -m 'not integration' --ignore=tests/test_report_pdf.py` — **1,245 passed, 17 skipped, 28 deselected**, same warning. `ruff check --select F` on the new/rewritten inheritance core, migration and new tests passed; broader changed legacy modules contain pre-existing lint findings. Python compilation and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Limitations/status:** **VERIFIED** that new results cannot produce/display heuristic inheritance scores or conclusions, and existing rows are migrated to review-required with scores removed. The data model still stores family genotypes, relationships and phase values, but scientific segregation, phase interpretation, de novo validation and ACMG evidence remain **NOT IMPLEMENTED/NOT VALIDATED** and require human review. No clinical validation or laboratory qualification is claimed.

### 2026-10-24 fail closed on malformed or incomplete population evidence in ACMG criteria

- **Finding:** `backend/app/acmg/evaluators.py` accepted any finite population AF, including negative values or values above 1.0; `int()` silently converted booleans and fractional values when reading AC/AN. BA1/BS1 could return `NOT_MET` when no selected population observation had a usable denominator, and PM2/BS1 could return a negative criterion result even when another selected population observation was unavailable or below the configured minimum AN. Since configured criteria considered `NOT_MET` are accounted for by ACMG orchestration, these cases could let incomplete population data be omitted from the combination step without forcing review.
- **Scientific basis:** ClinGen SVI's official PM2 recommendation downgrades generic rarity evidence to Supporting and explains that rarity/absence alone is weak because rare variants are common ([SVI PM2 recommendation, v1.0, approved 2020-09-04](https://clinicalgenome.org/site/assets/files/5182/pm2_-_svi_recommendation_-_approved_sept2020.pdf)). The active PAH CSpec illustrates that thresholds and population requirements are disease-specific: it specifies the highest-frequency subpopulation and minimum allele count and applies PM2 at Supporting ([ClinGen CSpec PAH](https://ldh.clinicalgenome.org/cspec/ui/svi/doc/1568416522)). This change implements only data-integrity and missing-evidence semantics; it does not set disease thresholds or claim PM2/BA1/BS1 are clinically validated.
- **Correction:** PM2 and BA1/BS1 now validate selected observations before use: AF must be finite and in `[0,1]`; counts must be true non-negative integers (not booleans/fractional values); AC cannot exceed AN; and any AF requires a positive AN. Invalid available metrics return `REQUIRES_REVIEW`; unavailable AF/AN or inadequate AN also returns `REQUIRES_REVIEW` when it prevents a criterion decision. A criterion can be `NOT_MET` only when the selected usable population data satisfy the configured minimum evidence requirements and remain below the configured threshold. PM2 defaults to Supporting strength per ClinGen SVI unless the selected disease-specific specification explicitly supplies another supported strength. Positive BS1/BA1 evidence and valid common-frequency PM2 counter-evidence retain their existing directions. Malformed observations outside the configured population scope do not block the criterion.
- **Regression coverage:** expanded `tests/test_criterion_evaluators.py` with 22 parameterized malformed-metric cases across PM2/BS1, out-of-range thresholds, missing denominators, missing selected populations, below-minimum AN, an explicitly excluded malformed cohort, and default versus explicit disease-specification PM2 strength.
- **Verification:** focused ACMG/API/workflow suite: `66 passed`, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Full non-integration backend suite: `1,276 passed, 17 skipped, 28 deselected`, one same warning; command excluded the native PDF rendering test and integration-marked tests. Ruff on both changed Python files passed; `py_compile` and `git diff --check` passed. No PostgreSQL integration, live resource, truth-set, or clinical validation was performed.
- **Status:** implementation and stated automated checks are **VERIFIED**. Criterion-specific threshold appropriateness, disease-specific CSpec qualification, independent benchmarks, and laboratory approval remain outside this change and **PARTIAL/NOT VALIDATED**. Phase A and laboratory deployment remain **PARTIAL**.

### 2026-10-24 require review when calibrated computational evidence is missing

- **Finding:** `evaluate_pp3_bp4` treated a missing or invalid configured predictor score as `NOT_MET`. The assessment service then combined other proposed criteria because it did not gate classification on evaluator or ClinGen source-assertion results already marked `REQUIRES_REVIEW`. Thus absence of a required calibrated predictor could be silently omitted from the automated classification.
- **Scientific basis:** ClinGen SVI's PP3/BP4 calibration work requires predictor-specific calibration and discusses risks from missing or outdated precomputed scores and identifier-mapping discrepancies ([Pejaver et al., 2022](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9748256/); [ClinGen SVI resource page](https://www.clinicalgenome.org/docs/calibration-of-computational-tools-for-missense-variant-pathogenicity-classification-and-clingen-recommendations-for-pp3-bp4-cri/)). A missing score is unavailable evidence, not a benign prediction or evidence that PP3/BP4 is not met.
- **Correction:** absent, null, nonnumeric, non-finite, or boolean predictor values now return criterion-level `REQUIRES_REVIEW` with `MISSING`/`INVALID` metadata. Invalid score intervals and strengths fail configuration validation. Assessment orchestration now blocks automated classification whenever an evaluator or ClinGen source assertion requires review, preserves independently supported proposals for reviewer context, and persists no classification row.
- **Regression coverage:** evaluator tests cover missing/null, malformed, NaN, infinity and boolean score values; a valid score outside the configured calibrated interval remains `NOT_MET`; invalid bounds, reversed intervals and unsupported strengths are rejected. An SQLite service-level test combines valid PM2 with missing PP3 and verifies review status, retention of the PM2 proposal, persisted PP3 review state, and absence of a classification row.
- **Verification:** focused ACMG suite after final test formatting: **74 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Full non-integration backend suite, run before formatting-only test cleanup: **1,288 passed, 17 skipped, 28 deselected**, one same warning; integration-marked tests and `tests/test_report_pdf.py` were excluded. `git diff --check`, Python compilation and Ruff F-code checks passed. Broader Ruff reports pre-existing import-order/modernization findings in the touched legacy modules; no full-file Ruff-clean claim is made.
- **Limitations/status:** automated safeguards are **VERIFIED** for deterministic evaluator/service fixtures. No predictor-specific scientific calibration, independent variant benchmark, live resource execution, or laboratory approval is established by this fix. Phase A, scientific validation and laboratory deployment remain **PARTIAL**.

### 2026-10-24 persist scientific resource discovery provider failures

- **Audit finding:** `discover_resource_candidates` caught a provider failure and returned only an aggregate `providers_failed` count. Because the discovery task is scheduled, provider execution or entry-point import failures had no durable operator-visible detail after the Celery result expired. A failure while loading one entry point also occurred before the per-provider isolation boundary and prevented other installed providers from running.
- **Correction:** resource provider entry points are now loaded inside each provider's isolated failure boundary. On provider load or discovery failure, that provider's candidate writes are rolled back, a `RESOURCE_DISCOVERY_PROVIDER_FAILED` event is committed to the existing hash-chained audit log, and other providers continue. The event records provider identity and exception type only; exception text is omitted to avoid persisting credentials or other sensitive values. Failure to persist the audit event propagates rather than being reported as successfully handled.
- **Regression coverage:** `tests/test_resource_discovery.py` injects a provider runtime failure and an entry-point import failure containing a secret-like message. It asserts durable audit records, omission of exception text, correct failure counts, and successful discovery of another provider after an import failure.

- **Operator visibility follow-up:** added `GET /api/v1/resources/provider-failures`, restricted server-side to `platform_admin`; it returns bounded, newest-first sanitized failure records from the audit chain and excludes arbitrary payload fields and exception text. Regression coverage verifies organization-admin denial and the exact sanitized response. The resource page now reads the backend-resolved session role and displays the failure history only for platform administrators, with localized English/Arabic labels and refresh/error/empty states; API authorization remains authoritative.
- **Verification of visibility:** focused authorization/discovery tests: **10 passed**; broader resource-discovery/registry/adoption/lifecycle/audit/queue/staging suite: **69 passed**. Each emitted one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Ruff `--select F,I`, Python compilation and `git diff --check` passed. Exact-SHA CI run [40077130916](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/40077130916) passed backend, frontend, container builds and PostgreSQL/Celery worker-loss integration for commit `fd59a26`. A broken database/audit store still makes the scheduled task fail and requires normal infrastructure recovery. ### 2026-10-24 bind HPO checksum verification to parsed ontology bytes

- **Finding:** the local HPO OBO provider hashed the ontology in one file read and parsed it in a second. A file replacement between those operations could make the provider execute ontology bytes that did not match the approved SHA-256 pin. The uncommitted parser refactor also had a syntax/indentation defect, which prevented workflow test collection.
- **Correction:** read the resource bytes once, calculate SHA-256 from that immutable in-memory snapshot, decode those same bytes as UTF-8, and parse the snapshot. File-read and decode errors become explicit `HPOOntologyError`s. The parser's validation and graph traversal remain within `_parse_text`; removed the now-unused path hashing helper. Added a regression that changes the file between attempted reads and proves parsed terms still come from the checksum-verified snapshot.
- **Verification:** `/tmp/siraloom-linux-test-env/bin/python -m py_compile backend/app/adapters/phenotype/hpo_obo.py tests/test_hpo_obo_provider.py` passed. Focused HPO/OBO/HPOA/phenotype suite: **52 passed**. Actual official HPO base ontology artifact at `/tmp/siraloom-hp-2026-09-01.obo`, SHA-256 `93dace952fcb3ec4728818857f6ba76bc2d5312f4d83266519b8694b8e798f22`, parsed as release `2026-09-01` with 20,482 terms; `HP:0001250` resolved to Seizure with four ancestors. `tests/test_actual_population_stage_redelivery.py`: **8 passed** after repair. Ruff on both changed Python files and `git diff --check` passed. Test runs emitted the existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning.
- **Integration gates:** implementation commit `8b19f126cc8635c64b18feea77bff0df4d4883c9` is present on remote `main`. Exact-SHA GitHub Actions run [40078308942](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/40078308942) passed backend, frontend, and PostgreSQL/Celery worker-loss jobs. The exact-SHA Vercel status completed successfully; `https://siraloom.vercel.app` returned HTTP 200. This deployment check does not establish laboratory backend deployment or scientific qualification.
- **Limitations/status:** checksum-to-parser integrity and deterministic ontology behavior are **VERIFIED** by the regression and official-release parse. The unrelated untracked `siraloom.egg-info/` was left untouched.

### 2026-10-24 require authentication for the production Redis broker

- **Finding:** the laboratory Compose profile launched Redis without authentication. Redis was not host-published, but every service on the Compose network could still connect without credentials; Redis carries Celery workload/control messages and results.
- **Research basis:** Redis documentation confirms `requirepass` causes clients to authenticate with `AUTH` and that the compatibility mode applies a password to the default user ([Redis AUTH](https://redis.io/docs/latest/commands/auth/), [Redis ACL](https://redis.io/docs/latest/operate/oss_and_stack/management/security/acl/)). The redis-cli health probe uses the documented `REDISCLI_AUTH` environment variable rather than putting the password in command-line arguments ([redis-cli docs](https://redis.io/docs/latest/operate/rs/references/cli-utilities/redis-cli/)).
- **Correction:** production Compose now requires `REDIS_PASSWORD`, configures Redis `requirepass`, runs an authenticated health probe, and injects the matching authenticated broker/backend URL into API, worker and Beat. Redis remains unpublished to the host. Production `Settings` rejects missing, malformed, or shorter-than-32-character Redis AUTH URLs; development keeps support for an unauthenticated local Redis. The production environment template and runbook now explain secure secret generation and external managed Redis configuration. CI Compose validation receives a disposable password.
- **Regression coverage:** deployment tests assert Redis is not host-published, the server and all three clients share the required credential, and the health check uses `REDISCLI_AUTH`. Settings tests cover unauthenticated and weak production credentials, authenticated `redis://` and `rediss://`, and unauthenticated development Redis.
- **Verification:** `PYTHONPATH=. DATABASE_URL=sqlite:// REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0 /tmp/siraloom-linux-test-env/bin/python -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp /tmp/siraloom-redis-auth-tests-final -q --tb=short tests/test_m7_deployment.py tests/test_laboratory_deployment.py tests/test_storage_capacity.py tests/test_resource_discovery.py` — **33 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Ruff on `backend/app/config.py` and `tests/test_m7_deployment.py`, Python compilation, and `git diff --check` passed. Local Docker and Redis executables are unavailable, so a local live Redis authentication test was not run. Exact-SHA GitHub Actions run [40079418085](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/40079418085) passed backend tests, PostgreSQL/Celery worker-loss integration, frontend production build/container, and production `docker compose config --quiet` validation on commit `1e5f5be38e5d7998f91129f664486d7db124449a`.
- **Limitations/status:** production configuration and startup guard are **VERIFIED** by deterministic tests; actual authenticated Redis service operation is not locally executed. Authentication does not provide Redis ACL command restrictions or encryption for in-network Compose traffic. Laboratory deployment, TLS/network qualification, Redis backup/recovery and the overall release remain **PARTIAL/BLOCKED** as appropriate.


### 2026-10-24 expand reanalysis reproducibility inputs and outputs

- **Finding:** `build_reproducibility_manifest` omitted important persisted analysis state: original input VCF identity; phenotype observations; variant and case technical-QC observations and assessments; segregation and inheritance; reportability and confirmation decisions; review actions; secondary-finding decisions; tool execution identity; workflow stage status/errors/decisions; and persisted stage artifact checksums. Two analyses could therefore receive `MATCH` while clinically meaningful evidence or workflow/report governance differed.
- **Correction:** the manifest now includes those persisted records and scientific analysis configuration. It resolves the case's `input_artifact_id`, requires a case-linked input with an accepted validation state and valid SHA-256 hex digest for eligibility, and includes its checksum/size/build/status in the output fingerprint. The random source artifact database ID is excluded from scientific identity. Stage input/output references include normalized persisted digests and compare declared digests; missing artifact rows, cross-case references, invalid digests and digest disagreements make the result ineligible instead of being misreported as a scientific mismatch. Review, classification, inheritance, segregation, QC, confirmation, reportability, secondary finding, tool and workflow decision outcomes are fingerprinted while volatile timestamps, row IDs and worker attempts remain excluded.
- **Regression evidence:** the persisted SQLite parent/child fixture proves equivalent results despite different analysis, artifact and result row IDs; it then independently changes source artifact digest, phenotype status, QC gate, tool version, analysis policy, segregation genotype, inheritance outcome, reportability approval, confirmation outcome, reviewer action, secondary-finding consent, workflow stage status and stage artifact digest, and confirms scientific changes cause `MISMATCH`. Invalid source/artifact digests and artifact checksum disagreements become `INELIGIBLE_INCOMPLETE_PROVENANCE`; missing resource pins remain `INELIGIBLE_UNPINNED_RESOURCES`. The CLI test invokes `main()` against persisted parent/child database rows for match, mismatch and ineligible outcomes.
- **Verification:** `PYTHONPATH=. DATABASE_URL=sqlite:// REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0 /tmp/siraloom-linux-test-env/bin/python -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp /tmp/siraloom-repro-manifest-final5 -q --tb=short tests/test_reanalysis_reproducibility_manifest.py tests/test_reanalysis_comparison_cli.py tests/test_reanalysis_reuse_provenance.py tests/test_case_change_reanalysis.py tests/test_reanalysis_resource_snapshot_pins.py` — **26 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Ruff on all four changed Python files, `py_compile`, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Limitations/status:** **VERIFIED** for the listed persisted SQLite fixture behavior and CLI service path. The CLI has not been run against real laboratory parent/child analyses or PostgreSQL; the fixture does not establish scientific equivalence of real analyses or qualification of resource pins. Phase A remains **PARTIAL**; deployed lab data, live tenant/storage/signout recovery, independent truth-set benchmarking and lab backup/restore remain unverified.

### 2026-10-24 snapshot pedigree context for segregation provenance

- **Finding:** segregation/inheritance comparison fingerprinted genotype, zygosity, phase and phenotype status but omitted the family-member relationships and pedigree graph that give those observations meaning. Pedigree members and relationships are case-scoped mutable records, so reading their current values during a later comparison would not reconstruct the context used when an observation was recorded.
- **Correction:** the authorized segregation write path now captures a server-generated, versioned snapshot of stable member identifiers, relationship-to-proband, sex, affected status, proband/sampled flags, specimen identifier/type, member metadata, and pedigree edges. The snapshot overwrites any client-supplied value in the reserved metadata key and is preserved by existing reanalysis reuse. The reproducibility manifest fingerprints it and marks legacy segregation observations without a version-1 snapshot ineligible, rather than guessing from current mutable pedigree rows.
- **Regression evidence:** tests assert stable sorted pedigree graph serialization, server authority over the snapshot, parent/child comparison change when the pedigree relationship changes, and ineligibility when the snapshot is absent. Existing case access, cross-case lineage, review and mutation authorization tests were included.
- **Verification:** `PYTHONPATH=. DATABASE_URL=sqlite:// REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0 /tmp/siraloom-linux-test-env/bin/python -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp /tmp/siraloom-pedigree-repro-final -q --tb=short tests/test_m10_pedigree_inheritance.py tests/test_reanalysis_reproducibility_manifest.py tests/test_reanalysis_comparison_cli.py tests/test_reanalysis_reuse_provenance.py tests/test_case_change_reanalysis.py tests/test_reanalysis_resource_snapshot_pins.py tests/test_audit_tenant_authorization.py tests/test_analysis_variant_access_guard.py tests/test_review_workflow.py tests/test_p0_mutation_authorization.py` — **127 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning.
