### 2026-10-24 correct directional HPO subsumption and expose reviewer context

- **Finding and execution path:** `EvidenceEngine.build_case_context_evidence` resolved a pinned HPO OBO file but built an ancestor map for disease terms. `match_hpo` then expanded those disease annotations and intersected them with patient terms. This could treat a broad patient observation as a match to a more specific disease annotation, while missing the converse (a specific patient term is compatible with a broader disease annotation). HPO defines child terms as more specific subclasses of their parents; the implementation had the subsumption direction reversed for patient-to-disease matching. This score is nonvalidated and does not feed ACMG classification or a calibrated diagnostic ranking.
- **Correction:** ontology-aware overlap now checks whether each present patient term equals or is a descendant of a disease-associated term using the pinned ontology's patient-term ancestor map. A broad patient term no longer implies a narrower disease annotation. The score semantics explicitly describe this relation and remain marked `clinical_prioritization_validated: false`. The clinical review evidence card now displays overlap status, matched patient terms, available score/reason, and a warning that this value is not a validated clinical prioritization score or diagnosis; English and Arabic strings are supplied.
- **Scientific basis:** official HPO documentation describes the ontology as a DAG with `is-a` subclass edges and more specific child terms; the documentation also notes that phenotype similarity algorithms vary and that an ontology overlap is not itself validation. See [HPO documentation](https://human-phenotype-ontology.github.io/documentation.html) and primary research describing HPO-based semantic matching and its limitations in [Encoding Clinical Data with the Human Phenotype Ontology for Computational Differential Diagnostics](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6814016/). The correction is a directional subsumption check, not an implementation or validation of Resnik, Phenomizer, BMA, or a clinical prioritization method.
- **Regression evidence:** pinned synthetic OBO tests cover broad patient term vs. narrow disease term (no match), specific patient term vs. broad disease term (match), ontology provenance, and rejection of caller-supplied hierarchy data. The real workflow redelivery fixtures were corrected to use an exact patient/disease phenotype term when expecting an overlap.

Frontend `npm run validate:i18n` passed; `npm run test:session` passed **34/34**; production `npm run build` completed with Next.js 15.5.24 on Linux Node 22.23.2/npm 10.9.8. Ruff `--select F,I` passed; `git diff --check` passed. Ruff format check on the entire evidence engine and large legacy test modules reports existing formatting differences outside the changed lines; no whole-file reformat was applied.

Exact-SHA GitHub Actions run [40084520420](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/40084520420) passed backend, frontend and PostgreSQL/Celery integration jobs, including backend/frontend container builds. ### 2026-10-24 reconcile reanalysis candidates at terminal provenance commit

- **Finding and execution path:** `run_variant_analysis` completes signed-out reanalysis through `_persist_terminal_provenance` in `backend/app/workflows/variant.py`. `backend/app/domain/reanalysis.py::mark_reanalysis_completed` existed but had no caller in the production workflow. Its selector also required candidate status `STARTED`. The candidate API recovery path can reset a candidate to `PENDING` after a queue exception while the linked child still proceeds; a successful child could therefore leave the candidate permanently pending with no completion audit.

Each completion event records the candidate's prior state. Replays remain idempotent because completed candidates are excluded from subsequent calls.

### 2026-10-24 mark case HPO terms unvalidated without a pinned ontology

- **Research basis:** the official HPO release publishes versioned ontology artifacts and phenotype annotations; the `phenotype.hpoa` contract identifies HPO terms and source/version context. See the [HPO release guidance](https://obophenotype.github.io/human-phenotype-ontology/developers/release/) and [official phenotype.hpoa format](https://obophenotype.github.io/human-phenotype-ontology/annotations/phenotype_hpoa/). An HPO-shaped identifier alone does not establish that it exists or is current in the analysis's approved ontology release.
- **Finding:** `EvidenceEngine.build_case_context_evidence` left `ontology_term_resolutions` null and `unresolved_terms_require_review` empty when no qualified HPO ontology was configured. Disease-level matching already withheld the score and returned `REQUIRES_REVIEW`, but case-only phenotype evidence did not explicitly identify each term as unvalidated, leaving the review record less clear than the configured-ontology path.
- **Correction:** case phenotype evidence now marks term validation `REQUIRES_REVIEW` with reason `PINNED_HPO_ONTOLOGY_UNAVAILABLE` and lists each original term with status `ONTOLOGY_UNAVAILABLE`. With a pinned ontology, resolved terms remain `VALIDATED` and unresolved terms retain their ontology-specific status. This does not rewrite submitted identifiers, create a score, or infer a diagnosis.

### 2026-10-24 execute phased multiallelic normalization fixture with pinned BCFtools

- **Research basis:** the official [BCFtools manual](https://samtools.github.io/bcftools/bcftools.html) documents `bcftools norm` reference-aware normalization and multiallelic splitting. The [HTS VCF specifications](https://github.com/samtools/hts-specs) define GT separators (`|` versus `/`), PS phase-set metadata, and allele-dependent INFO/FORMAT cardinality; the repository currently accepts VCFv4.2/v4.3 and does not claim v4.4/v4.5 compatibility.
- **Finding:** a prior golden fixture exercised one unphased `1/2` sample and checked Number=A/R/G cardinality, but did not execute the normalizer with multiple samples, phased calls, PS, or missing genotypes. Those are material representation semantics for downstream germline review.
- **Correction/evidence:** no normalization code change was needed. Added a production-path fixture for one multiallelic site with two phased samples (`1|2` and `2|0`) and one `./.` sample. The test checks sample order, allele-specific GT after splitting, retained `|` phase separators and PS values, and correct two-allele AD / three-genotype PL cardinality. Updated `docs/VARIANT_CLASS_SUPPORT.md` to distinguish this synthetic execution evidence from unverified broader phasing.
- **Tool environment:** the environment lacked BCFtools and `sudo` could not obtain a password. I downloaded the Ubuntu 24.04 repository packages into `/tmp`, extracted them to `/home/usama/.local/share/siraloom-tools/bcftools-1.19` without system or repository installation, matched the `.deb` SHA-256 to the apt repository metadata, and verified output `bcftools 1.19` / `Using htslib 1.19`.

This executed BCFtools normalization and production-wrapper record routing; `git diff --check`, Ruff `--select F tests/test_vcf_tools.py`, and Python compilation passed.
- **Limitations/status:** **VERIFIED** only for the deterministic tested representation cases. This does not validate segregation interpretation or clinical conclusions.

### 2026-10-25 validate specimen/sample identity before analysis readiness

- **Finding:** primary VCF intake checked file structure and selected assembly but did not compare the VCF header sample with the linked specimen's explicit `vcf_sample_name`. Analysis creation performed that check later, so an identity mismatch could be stored as `VALID` and make the case appear ready until analysis submission. A missing or malformed mapping had the same readiness inconsistency.
- **Correction:** intake now marks a VCF invalid unless its header contains exactly the explicitly mapped sample. It does not infer sample identity from the specimen identifier. Missing, blank, malformed and mismatched mappings produce persisted validation errors; the immutable source upload remains available, and the existing UI displays validation failure details. Assembly mismatch handling preserves all prior validation errors.
- **Related CI repair:** the PostgreSQL sign-out serialization fixture's eligibility stub did not accept the `report_type` keyword supplied by production code, masking a test `TypeError` as a timeout. The test now asserts the report type and surfaces thread exceptions. This was a fixture defect; production sign-out behavior was unchanged.
- **Verification:** focused intake/analysis suite — **53 passed**; full local backend suite before the final mapping-type parameterization — **1,376 passed, 46 skipped**; exact-SHA CI for `79db096` — all backend, frontend, PostgreSQL/Celery integration, image-build and Compose-validation jobs **passed**. Pinned BCFtools 1.19 production-wrapper normalization/routing suite — **66 passed**, including phased multiallelic, sample-order, PS, missing-genotype and Number=A/R/G assertions. The local PostgreSQL integration is skipped; its CI execution passed.

### 2026-10-25 require explicit rationale and signatory role in workspace report sign-out

- **Finding:** the workspace report action supplied a generic reviewer-approval phrase when the report-specific rationale was blank. That could make a report sign-out record appear to contain a considered authorization reason when the operator had supplied none. The workspace also displayed the action without waiting for and reflecting the current server session role.
- **Correction:** added a report-specific rationale field, disallowed blank/whitespace rationale in the client workflow, and gated the action on the role returned by `/auth/session`. The submitted reason is the user's trimmed report-specific text; the generic fallback translation was removed. The backend remains the authorization boundary and independently enforces signatory permissions. The control is fail-closed while the role request is unresolved or fails.
- **Regression coverage:** a shared permission helper now requires both a backend-aligned signatory role and nonblank rationale; tests exercise authorized roles with valid, blank and null rationale, and non-signatory roles with a supplied rationale. English and Arabic labels/help text were added and the translation architecture guard was run.
- **Verification:** `npm --prefix frontend run test:session` — **40 passed**; `npm --prefix frontend run validate:i18n` — **PASS**; `node frontend/node_modules/typescript/bin/tsc --noEmit --incremental false --project frontend/tsconfig.json` — **exit 0**; `npm --prefix frontend run build` using Linux Node 22.23.4 — **exit 0**, optimized production build completed and generated 24/24 static pages; `git diff --check` — **PASS**. The initial TypeScript invocation exposed the missing declaration for the new helper; the declaration was added and the independent TypeScript run and final build succeeded.
- **Limitations/status:** **VERIFIED** for the frontend authorization/rationale guard and build. No browser-driven sign-out, deployed identity service, actual laboratory artifact store, or end-to-end review-to-sign-out recovery was exercised in this task. Server-side signatory enforcement remains required. Phase A and laboratory/scientific release readiness remain **PARTIAL**.

### 2026-10-25 block automatic ACMG combination when evidence identity is reused

- **Scientific basis:** the official [ClinGen SVI specification registry, GN102](https://cspec.clinicalgenome.org/cspec/ui/svi/doc/GN102) warns that computational predictors with shared inputs must not be counted as independent criteria. The [ClinGen SVI specification record 1622722963](https://erepo.clinicalgenome.org/cspec/ui/svi/doc/1622722963) explicitly says not to count an individual literature-reported proband under both PP4 and PS4. These examples show that criterion combination requires evidence independence, not only valid criterion codes and strengths.
- **Finding:** `ACMGSpecificationAssessmentService` merged criterion proposals and passed them to the baseline combination engine without checking whether the same persisted `Evidence` identity appeared under more than one proposed criterion. The legacy `backend/app/domain/acmg.py` placeholder is not referenced by production callers; the defect was in the live specification assessment path.
- **Correction:** before combining criteria, the assessment service now groups proposed criteria by persisted evidence ID. If one evidence ID is used for multiple distinct criteria, it returns `REQUIRES_REVIEW`, preserves criterion/source assessment records and reason metadata, and does not persist an automated classification. No independence is inferred from a source record being attached to multiple criterion labels.
- **Regression evidence:** a persisted SQLite assessment fixture gives PS1 and PM5 separate ClinGen source assertions linked to the same Evidence row. It confirms that both proposals are retained, no classification is created, and the review reason identifies the reused evidence and both criteria. This test checks the actual assessment-service path and persistence operation.
- **Verification:** focused suite `PYTHONPATH=. DATABASE_URL=sqlite:// REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0 .venv/bin/python -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp=/tmp/siraloom-acmg-no-double-count-final -q --tb=short tests/test_specification_assessment.py tests/test_acmg_engine.py tests/test_criterion_evaluators.py tests/test_acmg_evidence_binding.py tests/test_reporting_signout.py` — **98 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Broader suite `tests/test_acmg*.py tests/test_clingen_selection.py tests/test_clingen_registry.py tests/test_specification_assessment.py tests/test_criterion_evaluators.py tests/test_review_workflow.py tests/test_reporting_signout.py` — **145 passed**, one existing deprecation warning. Ruff, Python compilation and `git diff --check` passed for the final changes.
- **Limitations/status:** **VERIFIED** for exact persisted Evidence-ID reuse on the tested service/persistence path. This does not detect semantically correlated evidence represented as distinct Evidence rows; that requires source-specific independence relationships and remains a scientific limitation. It does not validate the full ACMG implementation, any expert-panel specification, clinical classification accuracy, or laboratory sign-out. Phase A, scientific validation and deployment readiness remain **PARTIAL**.

### 2026-10-26 validate population AF against AC/AN at ingestion and evaluation

- **Finding:** local gnomAD and secondary-population VCF adapters accepted a reported AF that contradicted the selected ALT's AC/AN. The local gnomAD adapter also emitted source-rounded AF even when the same record supplied exact counts. Downstream PM2/BA1/BS1 validation checked AF's range and denominator but did not check consistency with persisted AC/AN, allowing inconsistent frequency evidence to influence a criterion.
- **Correction:** both local adapters now compare source AF to AC/AN within the decimal precision represented in the source field, reject contradictions, and persist the exact AC/AN ratio when counts are available. Secondary provider tests exercise the same source-rounding rule. PM2 and BS1 cases verify malformed count/frequency pairs require review.

### 2026-10-26 require exact consistency for integer-form source AF

- **Finding:** follow-up boundary review found the decimal-rounding calculation assigned an inappropriate 0.5 tolerance to a source AF written as integer-form `0`. That could admit `AC=1, AN=100, AF=0`, despite the source spelling carrying no fractional precision.
- **Correction:** both local population VCF adapters now allow rounding tolerance only when the AF text includes fractional decimal places; integer-form values must match AC/AN exactly. Fractional rounded AF remains accepted only within half of its represented last decimal place.

### 2026-10-26 prevent arbitrary VEP transcript selection from entering interpretation

- **Research basis:** Ensembl VEP returns transcript-level consequences, while the laboratory workflow research specifies retaining all consequences and recording the report transcript selection policy. MANE Select and MANE Plus Clinical are distinct transcript resources; the existing display policy uses a unique MANE Select transcript first, then a unique canonical transcript. A provider's response ordering is not a transcript-selection policy. See the official [VEP documentation](https://www.ensembl.org/info/docs/tools/vep/index.html) and [NCBI MANE resource](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/refseq/MANE/).
- **Finding and execution path:** `VEPProvider.normalize_result` selected the first transcript when none was marked MANE Select or canonical. If multiple candidates were marked, it selected the first according to response order. `ACMG assessment_service._variant_context` also fell back to the first consequence object whenever the normalized selected effect was absent. This allowed provider ordering to choose a single gene/transcript/consequence for downstream context.
- **Correction:** VEP selection now uses exactly one MANE Select candidate, or—only if there is no MANE Select candidate—exactly one canonical candidate. Multiple candidates or no preferred candidate leave the single-transcript fields empty, record an explicit selection status and policy, and preserve every transcript consequence. Malformed transcript consequence response structures are rejected. ACMG variant context no longer selects the first item from an unselected transcript list. The review UI displays selection status and exposes the preserved per-transcript consequences in a collapsible list with English/Arabic labels. The persisted report snapshot and rendered report now retain and show transcript selection status and selected transcript ID.
- **Regression and verification:** direct provider fixtures cover MANE precedence, no preferred transcript, ambiguous canonical candidates, ambiguous MANE Select candidates, malformed transcript consequence responses, and propagation of selection metadata through the VEP normalizer; assessment context verifies no arbitrary first-consequence fallback. Backend VEP/ACMG/evidence/annotation-redelivery/review/report contract suite — **122 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Report/sign-out-focused command (including rendered unresolved-transcript report) — **122 passed**, same warning. `frontend` `npm run build` passed the i18n guard, production compilation/type checks and static generation (24/24 routes). `npm run test:session` — **40 passed**. `ruff --select F`, `git diff --check` and Python compilation passed.
- **Limitations/status:** **VERIFIED** for deterministic provider, assessment-context, review payload UI build and regression behavior. No installed laboratory VEP/cache or real annotation fixture was available in this task; selected transcript policy is software behavior and does not establish clinical transcript appropriateness for each gene/disease. Phase A and scientific validation remain **PARTIAL**.

### 2026-10-26 require VEP binary and offline cache preflight before annotation

- **Finding:** the VEP provider accepted configured paths and a declared cache release, then proceeded directly to annotation. A missing binary, mismatched installed VEP release, unavailable cache, or assembly mismatch was not detected as a resource/runtime failure until scientific execution (or could be misconfigured entirely). Annotation provenance did not record evidence that the local tool/cache could start offline.
- **Correction:** the VEP annotation entry point now requires absolute binary/cache paths and a pinned GRCh37/GRCh38 assembly; rejects unsafe `extra_args` overrides of governed execution options; runs the configured binary's version command and checks it against the resource release; asks VEP to load the cache with offline/cache/cache-version/species/assembly options before annotation; rejects assembly disagreement; and records the binary digest, observed version, cache release, assembly and cache-info digest in the persisted annotation provenance. A provider instance reuses a successful preflight per assembly. Research/resource inventory now describes the unique MANE Select → unique canonical → unresolved policy and makes clear that a cache-info digest is not a checksum/qualification of all cache content.
- **Research basis:** official [Ensembl VEP cache documentation](https://www.ensembl.org/info/docs/tools/vep/script/vep_cache.html) describes cache release compatibility; official [VEP options](https://www.ensembl.org/info/docs/tools/vep/script/vep_options.html) document offline/cache execution and cache inspection. These runtime checks establish that the configured executable/cache can be queried offline, not the scientific correctness or qualification of every annotation.
- **Regression and verification:** `tests/test_vep_provider.py` now covers governed paths/assembly, successful version and offline-cache preflight, binary version mismatch, configured full-version mismatch, offline cache load failure, resource/analysis assembly mismatch, and runtime provenance. Focused Linux command `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp=/tmp/siraloom-vep-preflight-rerun -q --tb=short tests/test_vep_provider.py tests/test_resource_execution.py tests/test_resource_profile_registry_integration.py tests/test_actual_annotation_stage_redelivery.py tests/test_reporting_signout.py tests/test_reporting_contract.py` — **65 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. `git diff --check` passed. Targeted Ruff/compile checks are to be recorded after the final diff review.
- **Limitations/status:** no `vep` or `bcftools` executable, lab VEP cache, or real annotation fixture is installed in this WSL environment. Therefore actual binary/cache compatibility and scientific annotation remain **NOT RUN/BLOCKED**; mocked subprocess tests do not qualify them. The cache-info output digest is not a full cache content manifest. Provider preflight check is cached in-process and should be considered a point-in-time availability check; deployment resource governance still needs an immutable cache manifest/checksum and qualification evidence. Phase A, scientific validation and deployment readiness remain **PARTIAL**.
- **Next:** qualify a lab-provided VEP binary/cache pair using an immutable cache manifest and real known-answer variant fixture, then persist its qualification against the organization-approved resource binding. Do not treat the current mocked preflight as live resource qualification.

### 2026-10-26 require VEP offline-runtime preflight during registry qualification

- **Finding:** the existing resource qualification lifecycle verified generic source/staging metadata and artifact checksums but did not execute the VEP binary/cache contract. A VEP resource could therefore receive `QUALIFIED` status even if the binary was absent, its governed release differed, or the configured offline cache could not load; analysis would discover the failure only after dispatch.
- **Correction:** `qualify_resource` now invokes the existing VEP provider's governed runtime preflight for a staged VEP resource. The technical qualification record stores the runtime result (observed version, assembly, cache release, binary digest and cache-info digest). Missing staged resource artifacts and preflight failures become explicit activation blockers; they cannot produce a passed qualification. This is an execution availability check only and does not claim annotation accuracy.
- **Regression and verification:** `tests/test_resource_qualification.py` now exercises successful VEP version/cache preflight, binary release mismatch blocking, and absent staging blocking. Real VEP/cache qualification and a known-answer annotation fixture remain **NOT RUN**. The new check prevents false technical qualification for available failures but is not scientific qualification. ### 2026-10-26 pin installed VEP cache contents during preflight and registry qualification

- **Finding:** the preceding VEP preflight checked version and offline cache loading but did not verify the installed cache tree's bytes. A cache could change after approval, or contain unrecorded files, while the cache-info probe still succeeded. The registry checksum referred to the staged source artifact but was not bound to an inventory of the installed tree.
- **Correction:** the VEP contract now requires SHA-256 pins for the VEP executable, external cache manifest, and governed source cache artifact. The `siraloom.vep-cache-manifest.v1` manifest records release, species, assembly, source-artifact digest, and every cache-relative file path, size, and SHA-256. Runtime preflight checks the manifest and source-resource binding, rejects symlink/non-regular entries and missing, changed, or unlisted cache files, checks the executable digest before and after version/cache probes, then loads the cache offline. Runtime provenance records manifest, deterministic installed-tree, source-artifact, and binary digests. Registry qualification fails closed if the cache archive digest differs from the staged resource checksum. `scripts/generate_vep_cache_manifest.py` generates the inventory and refuses replacement absent explicit `--overwrite`; the research guide documents laboratory verification and approval. Preflight hashing reads the entire cache; laboratory-scale I/O cost is unmeasured.
- **Regression evidence:** tests cover deterministic manifest generation, no accidental overwrite, symlink rejection, cache-file drift, unlisted files, binary drift, resource/archive digest mismatch, and registry qualification. No test asserts annotation correctness from the manifest.
- **Verification:** Linux focused command `PYTHONPATH=. ### 2026-10-26 reject duplicate VCF record identifiers during intake validation

- **Scientific/format basis:** the supported VCFv4.3 specification says record IDs are unique and that an identifier must not occur in more than one data record ([HTS VCFv4.3 specification, fixed fields](https://github.com/samtools/hts-specs/blob/master/VCFv4.3.tex#L2705-L2712)). This repository's validator accepts VCFv4.2 and VCFv4.3.
- **Finding:** `validate_vcf_strict` only tracked identifier uniqueness while processing breakend records for `MATEID` checks. Duplicate identifiers on ordinary SNV/indel rows, or repeated semicolon-separated identifiers within a single record, passed validation.
- **Correction:** strict intake validation now rejects duplicate non-missing record IDs within one record and across all record classes with stable error code `VCF_ID_DUPLICATE`. The missing-value `.` is not entered into the uniqueness set. Existing breakend mate tracking continues to use IDs after the general check.
- **Regression and verification:** tests cover a repeated ID across two ordinary variant records and a repeated ID within one record's semicolon-separated list. Focused command `PYTHONPATH=. DATABASE_URL=sqlite:// REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0 .venv/bin/python -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp=/tmp/siraloom-vcf-id-unique -q --tb=short tests/test_vcf_validation.py tests/test_ingestion.py tests/test_vcf.py` — **74 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Broader VCF/normalization/routing command over those modules plus `tests/test_vcf_tools.py`, `tests/test_bcftools_normalization.py`, and `tests/test_unsupported_vcf_worker_routing.py` — **87 passed, 16 skipped** because BCFtools is unavailable locally. Ruff `--select F,I`, Python compilation, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Remote verification:** code commit `cdd320e66041538d1900f2b0b707ba774a5b28c3` is on `origin/main`; GitHub Actions run [40297986236](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/40297986236) passed backend, PostgreSQL/Celery worker-loss, frontend, BCFtools-enabled normalization, and container jobs.
- **Limits/status:** **VERIFIED** for this specified identifier uniqueness behavior and the covered intake paths. The broad VCF parser remains a hand-maintained structural/semantic validator; this fix does not establish complete VCF specification conformance. BCFtools golden checks remain skipped locally; remote CI is needed for that boundary. Overall Phase A and scientific/deployment readiness remain **PARTIAL**.

### 2026-10-26 handle valid reference-only VCF records without inventing variants

- **Scientific/format basis:** the supported VCF specification permits ALT `.` to mean there are no alternate alleles at the site ([HTS VCFv4.3, ALT fixed field](https://github.com/samtools/hts-specs/blob/master/VCFv4.3.tex#L2725-L2730)). This syntax must not be converted into a canonical variant or treated as a negative clinical interpretation.
- **Finding:** strict intake rejected `ALT=.` as malformed, while the lower-level variant iterator could parse it as an alternate allele if reached independently. The worker router also sent such records to the ordinary sequence normalization input.
- **Correction:** strict validation now recognizes a sole `ALT=.` as reference-only, validates it with zero alternate alleles for genotype/cardinality checks, and rejects `.` mixed with real ALT alleles. The parser skips reference-only rows. Mixed VCFs remain eligible when they contain variant records; intake reports the count and the original artifact remains immutable. The worker router counts and excludes these rows from normalization. A reference-only-only VCF is not accepted as a variant analysis and is routed to `UPSTREAM_VARIANT_CALLSET_REVIEW`; status text explicitly says it cannot establish a negative clinical result.
- **Regression and verification:** fixtures cover strict acceptance/rejection, mixed and all-reference-only intake, parsed-variant exclusion, router counts, and the real worker state/audit transition with source-artifact integrity. Linux adjacent VCF suite `PYTHONPATH=. DATABASE_URL=sqlite:// REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0 .venv/bin/python -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp=/tmp/siraloom-alt-dot-broad -q --tb=short tests/test_vcf_validation.py tests/test_ingestion.py tests/test_vcf.py tests/test_vcf_tools.py tests/test_bcftools_normalization.py tests/test_unsupported_vcf_worker_routing.py tests/test_normalization.py tests/test_normalization_artifact_idempotency.py` — **103 passed, 16 skipped**, one existing Starlette/httpx warning. The 16 skips are BCFtools-dependent because that executable is unavailable locally. Ruff `--select F,I` passed on changed domain/test modules; Ruff `--select I` passed on the large workflow module; Python compilation and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Limits/status:** **VERIFIED** for the specified `ALT=.` behavior through ingestion and the worker’s no-variant review boundary. The immutable original retains reference-only rows; no separate per-row derived artifact is generated for them. Local BCFtools execution is not available; the post-push CI run is required to exercise the configured BCFtools boundary. Phase A and scientific/deployment readiness remain **PARTIAL**.
- **Remote verification:** commit `e4c17b96a245d0f3721234fbcb61db4e9ed8f23c` is present on `origin/main`; exact-SHA GitHub Actions run [40299293365](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/40299293365) completed with conclusion **success** (backend, PostgreSQL/Celery recovery, and frontend/container jobs).

### 2026-10-26 accept and safely route VCF POS=0 telomere sentinels

- **Scientific/format basis:** the supported [HTS VCFv4.3 specification](https://github.com/samtools/hts-specs/blob/master/VCFv4.3.tex#L2696-L2703) explicitly permits POS 0 or N+1 to indicate telomeres. POS 0 is not a 1-based reference interval and cannot be sent through SIRALOOM's ordinary small-variant normalization path.
- **Finding:** strict intake and the low-level parser treated POS 0 as an invalid coordinate. If only the strict validator were relaxed, the reference validator would try to fetch a negative FASTA offset and the worker router could feed the sentinel to BCFtools as an SNV/indel.
- **Correction:** strict validation accepts nonnegative POS and counts `telomere_records`; intake keeps such a file valid while warning that these rows are excluded from variant analysis and need review. The parser excludes POS 0 from canonical small variants. Reference validation verifies that the contig exists, does not claim to check a POS 0 REF allele, and reports a `PARTIAL` profile. The worker emits immutable `TELOMERE_COORDINATE_RECORDS` artifacts, marks their REF-validation state `NOT_VALIDATED_UNSUPPORTED_COORDINATE`, excludes them from normalization, and routes telomere-only input to `VCF_TELOMERE_COORDINATE_REVIEW`.
- **Regression and verification:** focused command `PYTHONPATH=. DATABASE_URL=sqlite:// REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0 .venv/bin/python -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp=/tmp/siraloom-telomere-worker -q --tb=short tests/test_unsupported_vcf_worker_routing.py tests/test_vcf_validation.py tests/test_ingestion.py tests/test_vcf.py tests/test_vcf_tools.py` — **100 passed, 7 skipped**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Coverage includes strict acceptance/negative POS rejection, intake for telomere-only and mixed inputs, parser exclusion, partial reference-check result, router artifact creation, and the worker's durable review/artifact path. The seven skips are environment-dependent BCFtools cases.
- **Limit/status:** **VERIFIED** for POS 0 handling at the tested VCF intake, reference-check and worker-routing boundaries. POS N+1 is permitted by the format but is not yet explicitly routed using the selected contig length; it can still fail reference-coordinate validation. ### 2026-10-26 route VCF POS=N+1 sentinels using the pinned reference contig length

- **Scientific/format basis:** HTS VCFv4.3 permits POS 0 and N+1 telomere sentinels. N is the reference contig length; the VCF header can declare contig lengths, but the selected reference remains authoritative for the analysis.
- **Finding:** the prior POS=0 fix left N+1 unhandled. Header-declared N+1 could be routed as a sequence variant, while a sentinel without a declared contig length could reach REF validation at a coordinate beyond the reference. No-header lengths cannot safely be inferred until the approved reference is selected.
- **Correction:** parse `##contig` lengths, recognize a declared N+1 sentinel at intake/parser/router boundaries, cross-check declared lengths against the selected FASTA, then reroute after resolving the approved reference using its actual contig lengths. Preserve N+1 rows as `TELOMERE_AFTER_CONTIG_RECORDS`, mark them not REF-validated, and exclude them from BCFtools. Header/reference length disagreement blocks the reference validation path. The original VCF remains immutable.

### 2026-10-26 prevent retired ACMG/AMP PP5 and BP6 from influencing classification

- **Authoritative basis:** ClinGen's [Variant Curation SOP v1](https://clinicalgenome.org/site/assets/files/3677/clingen_variant-curation_sopv1.pdf), pages 39–40, says PP5/BP6 should not be applied in any context because primary evidence is increasingly available and the criteria are commonly misused; ClinGen's Variant Curation Interface disabled them. ACMG/AMP 2015 included the codes, but current SVI guidance informs this safety restriction.
- **Finding:** the generic combination engine accepted PP5/BP6; the CSpec snapshot retained them as executable structured rules; imported source assertions could be proposed at a configured strength; and the legacy `backend/app/domain/acmg.py` exposed a simplistic unversioned classifier. A ClinVar/ClinGen source assertion could consequently act as if it were primary evidence.
- **Correction:** remove PP5/BP6 from executable ACMG sets and strengths; make direct engine validation explicitly reject the retired codes; filter them from executable CSpec criterion mappings while retaining the raw CSpec response snapshot; preserve imported PP5/BP6 source assertions but mark their assessment `REQUIRES_REVIEW`, with no effective criterion/strength; and disable the legacy placeholder classifier so callers cannot obtain a classification from its simplistic rules.
- **Verification:** focused ACMG/ClinGen command covering engine, legacy path, API/resource binding, atomicity, evidence binding, specification assessment, CSpec registry/snapshot, source assertion assessment, criterion coverage, combination and merge modules — **65 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx warning. Full Python suite — **1,466 passed, 46 skipped**, same warning. Targeted Ruff `--select F,I`, Python compilation and `git diff --check` passed on the final code state.
- **Limits/status:** **VERIFIED** for automated exclusion, provenance preservation, and review-required routing in covered source-assertion flows. This does not validate other ACMG criterion evaluators, disease-specific specifications, classification truth sets, clinical validity, or laboratory authorization. Phase A, scientific validation and lab deployment remain **PARTIAL**. Commit, push and exact-SHA CI are pending.
- **Remote verification:** implementation commit `e35578752be7abe1976e0a7ab069dbbf76fe0851` is on `origin/main`; exact-SHA GitHub Actions run [40308277891](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/40308277891) completed **success** across backend tests with BCFtools, PostgreSQL/Celery worker-loss integration, and frontend production/container checks.

### 2026-10-26 persist review disposition for retired ACMG source assertions

- **Finding:** PP5/BP6 assertions were correctly preserved and blocked classification in the in-memory assessment result, but `ACMGSpecificationAssessmentService.persist` only wrote proposed criterion assessments. The explicit `REQUIRES_REVIEW` disposition therefore disappeared from the durable criterion assessment record, weakening reviewer visibility and recovery/audit traceability.
- **Correction:** persist non-proposed source criterion dispositions into the existing `ACMGAssessment` record, retaining source assertion IDs, selected specification identity/version, rationale and retired-criterion metadata. Review-required source evidence also sets the durable criterion state to `REQUIRES_REVIEW`. Repeated stage persistence replaces the matching source assessment rather than duplicating it. No classification is created from PP5/BP6.

### 2026-10-26 add verifiable laboratory backup and isolated restore tooling

- **Authoritative basis:** PostgreSQL 16 documents custom-format `pg_dump` archives for `pg_restore` reconstruction ([pg_dump](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/16/app-pgdump.html)); Docker Compose documents stopping and restarting services without removing them ([compose stop](https://docs.docker.com/reference/cli/docker/compose/stop/), [compose start](https://docs.docker.com/reference/cli/docker/compose/start/)).

Restore validates all digests and safe tar members, creates only a new `siraloom_restore_*` database and new storage paths, cleans up its newly created targets on failure, and never updates the live `.env` or starts writers. Compose project and environment-file options permit isolated restore instances. Installation documentation now describes setup, verification, isolation and operator evidence.

### 2026-10-26 surface versioned HPOA patient-context discrepancies for review

- **Research basis:** the official [HPO frequency guide](https://obophenotype.github.io/human-phenotype-ontology/annotations/frequency/) distinguishes the HPO Excluded term (`HP:0040285`, 0%), a finite-cohort count such as `0/3`, and percentages; it explains why `0/N` retains denominator-dependent evidence and should not be equated with an expert `NOT`. The official [HPOA format specification](https://github.com/obophenotype/human-phenotype-ontology/blob/master/docs/annotations/phenotype_hpoa.md) stores qualifier and frequency separately, defines aspect `P`, and notes that some similarity approaches restrict terms to that aspect. Sources accessed 2026-10-26.
- **Finding:** the production workflow persisted HPOA assertions and patient phenotype terms but did not compare them in the review dossier. Reviewers could inspect raw `NOT` and frequency fields, but potential overlaps between patient observations and disease annotations were not surfaced. Treating `0/N` as a disease-level negative would discard the cohort denominator and overstate the source assertion.
- **Correction:** the evidence builder now receives the patient HPO terms and pinned ontology from the actual analysis workflow. It stores a versioned, explicitly limited comparison for aspect `P` using exact/descendant `is_a` relations. Source `NOT`, HPO `HP:0040285` excluded frequency, literal `0%`, and finite-cohort `0/N` are distinct semantics. Unresolved ontology mappings, missing ontology, non-phenotypic aspects and patient term contradictions are retained as review context. The bilingual reviewer UI displays the comparison method and ontology release, source/patient terms, qualifier/frequency, unresolved/contradictory term identities and skipped-assertion count, plus a caution that this context is not a diagnostic conclusion, validated score or ACMG criterion. It does not alter candidate ranking or classification.

### 2026-10-26 pin multiallelic BCFtools genotype projection semantics

- **Research basis:** the official [BCFtools manual](https://samtools.github.io/bcftools/bcftools.html) documents `norm --multi-overlaps 0|.` for representing unselected alternate alleles after multiallelic splitting as reference or missing. SIRALOOM explicitly selects `0` and records that policy; this is a per-allele projection convention, not a claim that the original unsplit site lacked other ALT alleles.
- **Finding:** BCFtools golden coverage verified split variant coordinates and a separate GVCF wildcard path, but did not assert allele-indexed INFO/FORMAT remapping or phase preservation for a phased multi-sample multiallelic call. The tool's overlap behavior was implicit in its default.
- **Correction:** both existing split command paths now pass `--multi-overlaps 0`; normalization metadata records `REFERENCE_ALLELE_ZERO` and the actual command. A production-tool golden fixture now checks phased `1|2` projection, multi-sample genotypes, INFO `Number=A/R`, FORMAT `Number=R/G`, AC/AF remapping, PS retention, and the pinned policy.
- **Verification:** extracted Ubuntu package BCFtools **1.19** (with HTSlib 1.19) into `/tmp` without changing system packages. ### 2026-10-26 persist source allele lineage through normalization

- **Research basis:** the pinned official [BCFtools 1.19 manual](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/samtools/bcftools/1.19/doc/bcftools.txt) documents `norm --old-rec-tag` for retaining the original CHROM/POS/REF/ALT and the source ALT index used after splitting. The output uses a variable-cardinality string INFO value because the source ALT list contains commas.
- **Finding:** reference-aware normalization correctly changed coordinates and split multiallelic records, but discarded explicit source-record identity. The `CanonicalVariant` model had partial original-allele fields, yet the production BCFtools iterator did not populate them and analysis partition metadata did not persist them.
- **Correction:** the production wrapper adds a reserved `Number=.`/`Type=String` header to a private prepared VCF, inserts source mappings on biallelic rows (BCFtools may omit `--old-rec-tag` for unchanged records), and runs BCFtools 1.19 with `--old-rec-tag SIRALOOM_ORIG` for changed/split records. Multiallelic records are not pre-tagged, allowing BCFtools to attach each child's correct ALT index. A foreign/reserved-tag collision is rejected; preserving an existing SIRALOOM tag is an explicit option that validates the tag contract and per-record fields. Single-ALT tags where BCFtools omits USED_ALT_IDX resolve to index 1. Parsing rejects malformed or out-of-range indices. The iterator populates original allele fields; partition schema 3 persists per-variant source mapping. Normalization execution fingerprint and deterministic artifact identity move from v3 to v4 to prevent reuse of old bytes without mapping.
- **Verification:** actual BCFtools **1.19** and HTSlib **1.19** were extracted into `/tmp` without system package changes. Exact Linux command `PATH=/tmp/siraloom-bcftools-runtime/usr/bin:$PATH LD_LIBRARY_PATH=/tmp/siraloom-bcftools-runtime/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu PYTHONPATH=. DATABASE_URL=sqlite:// REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0 .venv/bin/python -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp=/tmp/siraloom-source-map-release -q --tb=short tests/test_vcf_validation.py tests/test_ingestion.py tests/test_vcf.py tests/test_vcf_tools.py tests/test_bcftools_normalization.py tests/test_unsupported_vcf_worker_routing.py tests/test_normalization.py tests/test_normalization_artifact_idempotency.py tests/test_m13_streaming_partitions.py` — **144 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. This includes the real production worker path persisting the two source mappings after GVCF wildcard trimming. Ruff `--select F,I` on BCFtools and test modules, Ruff `--select I` on normalization/schema/workflow modules, Python compilation and `git diff --check` passed. Initial focused runs exposed and corrected BCFtools' single-ALT tag form, unchanged-biallelic omission, and a mocked-command fixture that did not produce a compressed VCF.

This is lineage verification on synthetic fixtures, not independent truth-set concordance or laboratory qualification. Phase A and deployment readiness remain **PARTIAL**.

### 2026-10-27 reject ambiguous or malformed local gnomAD tabix responses

- **Research basis:** the GA4GH Large Scale Genomics [VCF 4.3 specification](https://github.com/samtools/hts-specs/blob/master/VCFv4.3.tex) defines eight mandatory fixed fields for each VCF record and a required integer POS. A query result that violates that shape cannot safely be interpreted as an allele absence. The specification allows repeated positions, so this check is specific to multiple records matching the same normalized allele, not multiple records at a locus generally.
- **Finding and correction:** the local gnomAD tabix adapter previously skipped records with fewer than eight fields, parsed POS with an uncaught `ValueError`, and stopped at the first exact allele match. A damaged or ambiguous local resource could therefore become a false `NOT_FOUND` result or arbitrarily select one evidence row. The adapter now reports malformed fixed fields, non-integer/negative POS and duplicate exact normalized-allele matches as `GnomADProviderError`; multiple distinct alleles at the queried locus remain valid.
- **Regression and verification:** `PYTHONPATH=. Ruff `--select F,I`, Python compilation and `git diff --check` passed. Tests specifically induce truncated fixed fields, malformed and negative POS, and duplicate exact allele rows.
- **Limits/status:** verified against deterministic tabix-output fixtures, not a locally installed/qualified gnomAD release or a clinical population-frequency benchmark. Local dataset identity, assembly, index, checksum and release qualification remain laboratory responsibilities. Scientific validation and laboratory readiness remain **PARTIAL**.

### 2026-10-27 replay profile-bound stages with the exact pinned qualification

- **Finding and execution path:** analysis preflight stores the selected resource qualification ID, version and execution-contract hash in `analysis.configuration.resource_plan.selected[].execution`. The profile runtime resolver previously called `resolve_resource_execution()` without that ID, which selected the latest `QUALIFIED` row and only then compared it with the analysis snapshot. Adding a newer qualification could therefore make a retry fail as `RESOURCE_PLAN_STALE`, even when the original qualification remained qualified. Using the newest qualification for an existing analysis would also violate its immutable scientific manifest.
- **Correction:** resource resolution now accepts an optional exact qualification ID. Profile-bound singular and plural runtime resolvers parse and pass the qualification ID from the saved plan. The query requires that exact ID to belong to the selected resource and still have `QUALIFIED` status. A missing, revoked or malformed pin fails closed; unprofiled legacy resolution retains the existing latest-qualified behavior.
- **Regression evidence:** SQLite-backed resolver test creates two qualified releases/contracts for one resource, confirms an unpinned discovery sees the new release while pinned replay obtains the original contract, and confirms revocation of that original pin fails without substituting. Runtime regression asserts the saved qualification ID is passed to execution resolution; existing stale-contract and tenant-boundary cases remain covered.
- **Verification:** focused resource execution/profile/planner/consumption suites — **48 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Full Linux backend suite `PYTHONPATH=. ### 2026-10-27 persist per-source-record VCF exclusion lineage

- **Finding:** routing preserved GVCF blocks, unresolved wildcard records, structural variants, spanning deletions, telomere sentinels and ALT='.' records in immutable source/routed artifacts, but only aggregate counts explained the routing decision. The normalized iterator separately removed homozygous-reference/reference calls, also with aggregate counts only. This made it difficult to review which exact source record or multiallelic ALT was omitted from candidate interpretation.
- **Correction:** `route_vcf_records` now streams a JSONL manifest for each excluded/routed source record. Entries identify the 1-based ordinal among non-header records, SHA-256 of the decoded source data line, coordinates/alleles, reason and destination class. For complete GT fields, it records each concrete source ALT index not called by any sample as `REFERENCE_ONLY_GENOTYPE`; missing or partial GT is not considered reference evidence. The worker persists the manifest as a content-addressed `VCF_EXCLUSION_MANIFEST` artifact linked to source artifact ID/checksum, includes the artifact in normalization step outputs and audit outputs, and verifies artifact bytes/provenance on retry.

### 2026-10-27 retain historical artifact readability across storage-profile changes

- **Finding:** the generic artifact download path resolved the active organization profile, then rejected a `file://` artifact whenever the current profile selected Firebase GCS. Case-export download dispatched through the currently active store instead of the artifact's immutable URI. The storage profile model documents that existing artifact URIs remain immutable, so switching the active profile could make intact historical bytes inaccessible.
- **Correction:** add `artifact_store_for_uri`, which still enforces required profile presence but chooses only the configured deployment local root or configured Firebase bucket by persisted URI scheme. Both generic artifact and case-export downloads now use it, then verify the canonical URI is bound to the case/artifact/filename and verify bytes against SHA-256 and size. Tenant authorization remains before storage resolution. Local artifact responses now include `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`.
- **Regression and verification:** Linux storage profile, generic artifact download identity/integrity, case-export download/integrity and tenant-guard suites — **28 passed, 1 skipped**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Covered profile switched to cloud while reading old local URI, current cloud configuration unavailable while local artifact remains accessible, configured cloud URI, invalid schemes, canonical URI binding, corrupt-byte rejection and auth-before-store resolution. Ruff `--select F,I`, Python compilation and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Status/limits:** **VERIFIED** for URI-pinned backend selection and integrity controls in local automated tests. Local root and Firebase bucket remain deployment-wide settings; distinct organization-owned physical targets and historical bucket-version mappings remain unsupported. No live bucket/mount or deployed multi-lab test was run. Phase A and lab deployment remain **PARTIAL**; exact-SHA CI is pending.

### 2026-10-27 isolate Firebase Storage credentials per deployment target

- **Research:** official Firebase Admin Python docs confirm `firebase_admin.initialize_app(..., name=...)` supports multiple named app instances and that `firebase_admin.storage.bucket(name, app=...)` binds a bucket client to a selected app. Sources: [Admin SDK Python reference](https://firebase.google.com/docs/reference/admin/python/firebase_admin) and [Storage module reference](https://firebase.google.com/docs/reference/admin/python/firebase_admin.storage), accessed 2026-10-27. The repository dependency allows `firebase-admin>=6.6,<7`; the executing Linux virtualenv has **6.9.0**, so this is not an exact repository pin.
- **Finding:** all Firebase artifact stores used the Firebase Admin default app, credential path, project ID and bucket. The default app is also used by Firebase token verification; storage therefore could not have a separate lab-owned identity and named storage apps could suppress the old `_apps`-based default-auth initialization check.
- **Correction:** deployment target entries may now specify `project_id` and an absolute `credentials_path` alongside the bucket and owning organization UUID. These values remain deployment configuration and are not copied into profile JSON or API payloads. Explicit targets use deterministic, separately named Firebase apps, a target-specific service-account credential file when supplied, or Application Default Credentials without inheriting the Firebase Auth credential file. Storage bucket access is requested through the target app. Auth now checks initialization of the default Firebase app specifically and safely initializes it even when named storage apps already exist. Added a Compose secrets override example for mounting the credential file read-only into API and worker. Credential/config rotations require service restart to replace cached Firebase apps.
- **Regression and verification:** focused Firebase, target profile, auth-default-app, artifact cleanup/download, case-export and tenant-guard suites on the final code — **57 passed, 1 skipped**, one existing Starlette/httpx warning. The full Linux backend suite immediately before the final app-name fingerprint fallback refinement — **1,465 passed, 48 skipped**, same warning. Ruff `--select F,I`, Python compilation and `git diff --check` passed on the final code. Changed compose-example YAML parsed locally and its API/worker read-only secret targets were checked; Docker/Compose itself is unavailable. Tests mock Firebase Admin initialization to verify separate app objects, credential paths, project IDs, bucket binding, ADC use, and the auth default-app regression; they do not call live Firebase.
- **Status/limits:** **PARTIAL**. No real service-account files, live Firebase projects/buckets, storage IAM policies, cross-lab cloud access denial, network/firewall behavior, or Compose deployment were exercised (Docker is unavailable in this environment). The project dependency is a range, not exact version-pinned. A shared ADC identity remains possible when the lab omits `credentials_path`; independent lab identity requires a separately mounted credential file and least-privilege bucket IAM. These changes are local/uncommitted and have no exact-SHA CI result yet. Overall Phase A and laboratory deployment remain **PARTIAL**.

### 2026-10-27 enforce organization-bound artifact storage on upload and worker reads

- **Finding:** artifact intake's `_store` consulted an organization profile only to check that one existed, then constructed the adapter from deployment-global root/bucket settings. The workflow worker likewise downloaded every `gs://` artifact through the global Firebase bucket and resolved local `file://` URIs without checking the owning case's organization. This bypassed the recently added per-organization target bindings and could send a laboratory write to the wrong target or allow a worker to read an artifact URI outside that case's assigned storage.
- **Correction:** intake now delegates adapter construction to `artifact_store_for_organization`. Worker materialization loads the artifact's case, resolves the persisted URI through `artifact_store_for_uri` for that case's organization, verifies local URI containment using the returned adapter, and retains cloud checksum/size verification before staging bytes. Missing cases, unassigned URIs and adapter/scheme mismatches fail closed. Annotation response replay uses the same path.
- **Regression and verification:** full Linux backend suite using isolated SQLite — **1,467 passed, 48 skipped**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Focused storage/materialization/download suite — **25 passed**. Ruff import checks, changed-file F401 checks, Python compilation and `git diff --check` passed. Regression fixtures now model organization resolution for uploads and worker reads; existing storage-profile tests cover cross-organization URI denial and distinct target bindings.
- **Status/limits:** implementation and automated verification are **VERIFIED** for local adapter/configuration and SQLite workflow scope. Live two-laboratory storage, Firebase IAM, separate service identities, cloud downloads, and backup/restore remain NOT RUN. The first exact-SHA run [40478625123](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/40478625123) passed backend and frontend but failed the PostgreSQL GVCF worker integration because that fixture created a temporary store without an organization storage profile. The fixture is corrected below; a passing exact-SHA rerun is required. Phase A and laboratory deployment remain **PARTIAL**.

The local environment lacks a PostgreSQL integration URL, so this test is **SKIPPED locally**; Ruff import/F401 checks, Python compilation and `git diff --check` pass. Exact-SHA GitHub Actions run [40479129831](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/40479129831) passed all jobs on commit `86e6208df7691dc0c102eeca88e82f608c632ed7`, including PostgreSQL/real Celery recovery with pinned BCFtools, backend/container checks, and frontend/production Compose checks.

### 2026-10-27 mount organization-specific local storage targets in laboratory Compose deployments

- **Research basis:** official [Docker Compose service/volume documentation](https://docs.docker.com/reference/compose-file/services/) defines bind mounts as host paths mounted at explicit container targets and documents long syntax, including `create_host_path`. The SIRALOOM target registry resolves local `root` inside each container; its production Compose file previously mounted only the legacy `/data/siraloom/artifacts` location.
- **Finding:** administrators could configure `/data/siraloom/labs/<lab>/artifacts` as an organization-bound target root, but the production API and worker services had no bind mount for that path. A valid target/profile could therefore become unwritable or reference a container-local directory instead of the lab's durable host storage. Existing docs showed host-looking roots without explaining the container path/mount mapping.
- **Correction:** add `deployment/storage-targets-local.compose.example.yml` with one dedicated bind source per sample lab and matching API/worker mounts, using `create_host_path: false`. Update the deployment guide and `.env.production.example` to distinguish host path variables from container-side JSON roots, document the merged Compose invocation, and clarify that the base artifact volume is legacy/single-root. Add a test for matching mounts in API/worker, and extend GitHub Actions to run `docker compose config --quiet` with the override.
- **Verification:** `tests/test_m7_deployment.py` — **13 passed**; Ruff `--select F,I` and `git diff --check` passed. WSL has no Docker/Compose binary, so merged Compose execution was not run locally; exact-SHA GitHub Actions run [40484691857](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/40484691857) passed frontend, backend, PostgreSQL/Celery integration, image builds and production Compose validation for commit [`e17ab2a84f153b1b704c157e26006a9384bbf60e`](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/commit/e17ab2a84f153b1b704c157e26006a9384bbf60e), including the merged override configuration.
- **Status/limits:** **IMPLEMENTED, PARTIAL verification**. The override provides the required mount wiring but does not qualify ownership/mode, host backup, disk capacity, SELinux behavior, actual Docker startup or cross-lab operational permissions. Overall laboratory deployment readiness remains **PARTIAL**.

### 2026-10-27 use organization-bound artifact roots for capacity and readiness

- **Finding:** local artifact targets are selected per organization, but upload and analysis capacity estimates, the laboratory capacity endpoint, and `/ready` inspected only the global legacy `ARTIFACT_ROOT`. A target could be missing or full while the unrelated global root appeared healthy. With capacity preflight disabled, `ArtifactStore` could also create a missing configured target inside a container's ephemeral writable layer. Separately, readiness wrote to a fixed filename, overwrote any existing file at that path, then deleted it.
- **Correction:** added one organization-scoped target resolver and use its backend/location for upload and analysis capacity preflights and the authenticated storage-capacity endpoint. Local targets are measured at their selected path. Cloud targets no longer inherit local free-space figures; their estimated output is explicitly labeled provider-managed and not measurable as filesystem capacity. Configured local roots must already exist and be writable before store construction; they are never auto-created. `/ready` tests local target roots without creating missing directories and uses a unique, fsynced temporary probe with cleanup rather than a predictable shared filename.
- **Regression and verification:** the focused Linux set covering storage capacity, deployment readiness, storage profiles, resource dispatch, artifact materialization, ingestion, upload policy, report signout, report artifact replay/compensation, annotation/population worker replay, report-stage reexecution and case-export recovery — **142 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. The full backend suite, using an isolated `/tmp` SQLite database so the pre-existing untracked `ci.db` was untouched, passed **1,525 tests, 48 skipped**, with the same warning. Ruff `--select F,I`, Python compileall on changed runtime modules and `git diff --check` passed. Exact-SHA GitHub Actions [40487483258](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/40487483258) passed backend, PostgreSQL/Celery integration, frontend/container, and merged production Compose validation on commit [`6944c409ea8455d12dcfd08dd929266ce385697f`](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/commit/6944c409ea8455d12dcfd08dd929266ce385697f).
- **Limits/status:** **VERIFIED** for local automated capacity/root/readiness contracts and cloud-capacity labeling. No real mounted lab filesystem, Firebase bucket, storage quota exhaustion, backup/restore, or deployed readiness test ran. Cloud object-store write/read/delete reachability is not probed by `/ready`; actual operations still fail at the provider write boundary. Capacity estimates do not reserve bytes. Phase A and laboratory deployment remain **PARTIAL**.

### 2026-10-27 probe the configured Firebase artifact target during readiness

- **Finding:** readiness skipped organization-bound cloud targets and always probed the legacy local artifact root. A cloud-only laboratory could therefore be marked ready despite a broken bucket, or marked not-ready because an unrelated local root was absent. Merely constructing the Firebase bucket handle makes no provider RPC, so it would not establish connectivity or storage permissions.
- **Research:** official [Firebase Admin Python Storage reference](https://firebase.google.com/docs/reference/admin/python/firebase_admin.storage) states that `storage.bucket()` returns a handle without making RPCs. The official [Google Cloud Storage Python Blob reference](https://docs.cloud.google.com/python/docs/reference/storage/latest/google.cloud.storage.blob.Blob) documents `upload_from_string`, `download_as_bytes`, `delete`, timeouts, and generation-match preconditions; generation `0` prevents replacing a live object. Accessed 2026-10-27.
- **Correction:** Firebase artifact stores now provide an isolated write/read/delete probe using a random object path, short per-operation timeout, create-only generation precondition, generation-pinned read/delete, content verification, and best-effort cleanup if a stage fails. Readiness invokes that probe for configured organization cloud targets and for the legacy cloud target when no explicit target map exists; it skips the unrelated legacy local-root check when explicit targets are configured. Successful and failed probe outcomes are cached briefly (30 seconds and 5 seconds respectively) to avoid health-check request storms. Provider exceptions and bucket/credential details are not exposed in the response.
- **Verification:** `tests/test_m7_deployment.py tests/test_storage_profiles.py tests/test_firebase_artifact_streaming.py tests/test_firebase_artifact_integrity.py tests/test_artifact_store_flush_cleanup.py` — **44 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. The full backend suite with an isolated `/tmp` database passed **1,529 tests, 48 skipped**, with the same warning. Ruff `--select F,I`, Python compileall on changed runtime modules, and `git diff --check` passed. Tests use deterministic provider fakes; no live Firebase bucket was contacted.
- **Status/limits:** the probe tests code paths and permission behavior only when executed against configured infrastructure. It adds a small temporary-object operation to cloud readiness; the cloud account must permit object create, read, and delete under `_siraloom/readiness/`. A live bucket, object-retention policy, IAM role, and deployed health check remain unqualified. Overall laboratory deployment and Phase A remain **PARTIAL**.

### 2026-10-27 require reviewed, traceable confirmation decisions before report release

- **Finding:** case-write roles could set `required=false`, `COMPLETED`, or `WAIVED` on a confirmation record; the endpoint stamped the submitter as `reviewed_by` regardless of role or decision state. Report eligibility accepted `COMPLETED`/`WAIVED` based on the string alone and accepted unresolved `required=false/PENDING` records. The report snapshot omitted reviewer identity/time, so sign-out could not verify that provenance.
- **Correction:** confirmation writes that clear or waive the gate (or state confirmation is not required) now require case review permission and a `REVIEW_ROLES` principal. Case-write roles may record only a required pending/failed status. An explicit `NOT_REQUIRED` decision requires rationale; `WAIVED` requires rationale; `COMPLETED` requires method, result, laboratory, accession and timezone-aware performed time. Only reviewer-approved terminal decisions receive `reviewed_by`/`reviewed_at`. Report eligibility rechecks those constraints and blocks unresolved or malformed records; report snapshots now preserve confirmation review provenance, and finalization rejects stale reviewer identity/time. The review UI follows the reviewer-role boundary, defaults confirmation to required, and captures performed-at time.

Full Linux backend suite on isolated SQLite — **1,501 passed, 48 skipped**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning, in 273.68s. Frontend `npm run test:session` — **41 passed**; `npm run validate:i18n` passed; Linux Next.js 15.5.24 production build, compilation, type check and static page generation passed. Python compilation, Ruff F/I checks on the new route and directly changed test module, and `git diff --check` passed. Three legacy import-order Ruff findings in reporting files are unchanged from `HEAD`.
- **Limits/status:** **VERIFIED** for the automated route and report-gate behavior on SQLite plus frontend build/tests. No deployed laboratory policy/SOP, PostgreSQL sign-out concurrency, live browser, or real storage was exercised in this batch. Historical unresolved records now block sign-out and require a reviewer-created explicit decision. Phase A and laboratory deployment remain **PARTIAL**. ### 2026-10-27 require an explicit confirmation disposition for reportable findings

- **Finding:** the prior report gate validated confirmation status only when a `ConfirmationRecord` existed. A reportable finding with no record bypassed both the confirmation check and any explicit laboratory decision that confirmation was unnecessary. This conflated "no decision recorded" with "not required".
- **Correction:** clinical report eligibility now requires each reportable finding to have a versioned confirmation decision. Existing reviewed `COMPLETED`, reasoned `WAIVED`, and reasoned `NOT_REQUIRED` decisions remain eligible under their previously defined validation; absent, pending, failed, inconsistent, or unreviewed decisions block report generation/sign-out. The policy does not assert that orthogonal confirmation is universally required; the reviewer must explicitly record the laboratory disposition.
- **Regression and verification:** focused report/sign-out/contract tests — **50 passed**; connected reportability, sign-out, resume, reviewer integration and confirmation persistence suites — **85 passed**. The report-generation route invokes this same eligibility gate before creating drafts.
- **Status/limits:** **VERIFIED** for local automated report eligibility/finalization behavior. No laboratory SOP configuration is inferred; explicit decisions remain human-entered and role-gated. PostgreSQL concurrency, browser flow, deployed identity/storage and clinical validation are not established by this test run. Phase A and lab deployment readiness remain **PARTIAL**. ### 2026-10-27 bind report sign-out to exact clinical reviewer decisions

- **Finding:** the draft classification digest omitted the approving reviewer and approval timestamp. Sign-out only checked the reportability decision identity/version/final state, not its disposition, rationale, policy, or reviewer identity/time; reportable secondary-finding snapshots likewise omitted and did not revalidate their reviewer provenance or decision content. A modified decision could therefore retain the same record/version and still pass snapshot validation.
- **Correction:** classification snapshots now bind `reviewed_by` and `approved_at`; reportability snapshots include and revalidate the exact classification link, disposition/status, reasons, policy/version, review version, reviewer and approval timestamp; reportable secondary-finding snapshots include and revalidate policy, eligibility, consent, disposition, rationale, context, reviewer and review time. Timestamp serialization normalizes naive database timestamps as UTC and aware values to UTC, so snapshots compare consistently after SQLite/PostgreSQL persistence.

### 2026-10-27 bind immutable report drafts to their persisted JSON content

- **Finding:** report generation rendered a PDF from `Report.content_json` and stored the PDF artifact, but the artifact did not bind to a digest of the JSON. Sign-out later rendered the final PDF from the mutable JSON. Changes to fields not covered by the specialized clinical decision checks could therefore make the signed PDF differ from the original draft without invalidating the draft artifact.
- **Correction:** new report drafts record a versioned canonical-JSON SHA-256 in immutable draft artifact metadata. Draft artifact validation now checks the digest; protected report access and finalization reject mismatched content or unsupported digest formats. Drafts predating this format retain the prior lineage and decision-snapshot checks and are not rejected solely for lacking the marker.
- **Regression and verification:** tests verify that the report creation route stores the exact digest and that changing report content after draft generation blocks sign-out. Focused report artifact, sign-out, reportability and recovery suites — **81 passed**. Full Linux backend command `PYTHONPATH=. DATABASE_URL=sqlite:////tmp/siraloom-report-content-integrity-full.sqlite REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0 GENEBE_ENABLED=false GNOMAD_ENABLED=false FIREBASE_AUTH_REQUIRED=false .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp=/tmp/siraloom-report-content-integrity-full-tests --tb=short tests` — **1,508 passed, 48 skipped**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Ruff F/I checks, Python compilation and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Status/limits:** **VERIFIED** for digest persistence/validation and local SQLite report/sign-out behavior plus exact-SHA GitHub Actions run [#40503163556](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/40503163556) for `6ce86caa7cab0bca987b008a9c87ca746f713f2c`; backend, frontend, PostgreSQL/Celery worker-loss integration, container builds, BCFtools verification and production Compose checks passed. Existing legacy drafts without the digest remain subject to their earlier narrower field-by-field checks. The digest is integrity metadata stored alongside the artifact, not a signature against a privileged database operator. No deployed object-store tamper test, browser flow, laboratory qualification, or clinical validation was performed. Phase A and laboratory deployment remain **PARTIAL**.


### 2026-10-27 reject sign-out when rendered report source records have changed

- **Finding:** the report-content digest detects edits to persisted report JSON, but case/analysis sources can change after a draft is rendered while that JSON and digest remain untouched. The report includes clinical indication/context, phenotype, pedigree, variant representation, annotation, population/evidence, inheritance/segregation, confirmation and follow-up projections; not all of those sources had individual sign-out checks.
- **Correction:** sign-out now rebuilds the report content from current persisted case/analysis records and compares it to the stored draft after the existing targeted identity/decision checks. The comparison normalizes the allocated report version and omits only generated time, audit summary, and the current report's own draft artifact entry, which are expected to change during report creation/sign-out. A mismatch blocks sign-out and directs regeneration.

### 2026-10-27 serialize report-source mutations against sign-out

- **Finding:** `finalize_report` locks the report, analysis and case before validating persisted source content. Phenotype, specimen and pedigree-member writes did not all use the case lock; segregation and inheritance writes did not use the analysis lock. Such writes could commit after sign-out's source-snapshot comparison but before the signed report transaction committed.
- **Correction:** case-level phenotype, specimen and pedigree-member writes now lock and refresh the case row before mutation; pedigree relationship edits already used that lock. Segregation and inheritance-assessment writes now lock the analysis row and recheck case permission before reading inputs or persisting report-contributing output. This shares the same row-lock boundaries that report finalization acquires.
- **Regression coverage:** unit fixtures assert case lock/refresh on phenotype, specimen and pedigree-member writes, and analysis locking/permission scope on pedigree assessment paths. A PostgreSQL integration test holds the case lock and invokes the actual phenotype endpoint on a separate session, asserting the write remains blocked until lock release and then persists. Existing read-only authorization regressions remain covered.
- **Verification:** focused local report-source, phenotype, specimen, pedigree, authorization and variant-access suites — **91 passed, 1 skipped**; the skip requires PostgreSQL. Full Linux backend suite — **1,511 passed, 51 skipped**. Ruff F/I on changed application modules and focused test files, Python compilation and `git diff --check` passed. Ruff F/I on `tests/test_audit_tenant_authorization.py` still reports four pre-existing findings (one import-order and three unused imports outside this patch). Exact-SHA Actions run [#40511867170](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/40511867170) completed successfully on `c9935af`, including PostgreSQL/real Celery integration **31 passed**, backend tests/container build and BCFtools version verification, frontend behavior/build/container, and production Compose validation.
- **Status/limits:** **VERIFIED** for the exercised row-lock boundaries and PostgreSQL phenotype-writer contention. The PostgreSQL test executes the actual phenotype route; specimen/member and segregation/inheritance route coverage asserts lock acquisition in unit tests. Deployed laboratory sign-out, clinical validation or browser journey is not implied. Phase A and laboratory deployment remain **PARTIAL**.

### 2026-10-27 serialize manually curated evidence against sign-out

- **Finding:** report content includes manually curated LITERATURE, GENE_DISEASE and PHENOTYPE evidence, but `POST /analyses/{analysis_id}/variants/{variant_id}/context-evidence` inserted evidence without locking the analysis row. A reviewer could add evidence after report snapshot verification but before sign-out committed.
- **Correction:** after authorization and variant lineage checks, the route now locks and refreshes the analysis, reloads its owning case, and rechecks write permission before reading the annotation or creating the evidence row. This uses the same analysis-row serialization boundary as sign-out.
- **Regression coverage:** added unit coverage for lock/refresh/permission recheck and a PostgreSQL integration test that holds the analysis lock while invoking the real context-evidence endpoint on another session; the evidence write must wait, then persist after lock release. Existing cross-analysis variant and read-only access regressions remain in the focused suite.

- **Status/limits:** **VERIFIED** for the endpoint lock boundary and PostgreSQL contention behavior.
