This does not establish clinical validity, live provider qualification, deployed lab behavior or report authorization in a laboratory environment. Phase A and laboratory deployment remain **PARTIAL**.

### 2026-10-27 serialize report draft generation against analysis decisions

- **Finding:** report draft creation serialized report-version allocation on the case row but did not lock its Analysis row. Classification, reportability, QC, evidence and governance writers use the analysis lock, so a draft could read report inputs while those records changed. Sign-out would later reject a stale snapshot, but reviewers could inspect an internally inconsistent draft before regeneration.
- **Correction:** report creation now locks and refreshes Analysis before locking and refreshing Case, then rechecks case write permission. The order matches sign-out and protects both analysis-level decisions and case-level report inputs through draft persistence.
- **Regression coverage:** the report-generation compensation unit fixture asserts the sequence Analysis lock/refresh → Case lock/refresh → permission recheck → eligibility. Added PostgreSQL integration coverage that holds the Analysis row lock and invokes the actual report-generation route, asserting it does not reach eligibility or persist a draft until the lock is released.
- **Verification:** focused report/sign-out/contract/recovery suite — **53 passed, 1 skipped** (PostgreSQL unavailable locally). Ruff F/I, Python compilation and `git diff --check` passed. Exact-SHA Actions run [#40514057686](https://github.com/SayedaRehmat/SIRALOOM/actions/runs/40514057686) passed for `5a778448639a8109646f88dfd50068df927d7bd6`; PostgreSQL/real Celery integration reported **33 passed**, backend **1,578 passed, 34 skipped**, and frontend behavior/build/container plus backend image, BCFtools, and production Compose checks passed.
- **Status/limits:** **VERIFIED** for draft-generation concurrency in PostgreSQL CI. The test uses a controlled PDF/storage adapter and does not qualify deployed laboratory storage. Phase A and laboratory deployment remain **PARTIAL**.

### 2026-10-27 serialize follow-up plan writes against report sign-out

A plan could therefore be inserted or changed after sign-out reconstructed the report source snapshot and before its transaction committed.
- **Correction:** follow-up creation now locks and refreshes the Analysis and rechecks case write permission before inserting. Update locks the owning Analysis, reloads the plan after acquiring the lock, then validates its case and variant lineage before mutation. These writes share the report-generation/sign-out serialization boundary.
- **Regression coverage:** existing follow-up persistence and authorization tests exercise create/update behavior and case-link rejection. ### 2026-10-27 preserve before-and-after state for follow-up edits

- **Finding:** follow-up plan updates recorded only `after_state` in the audit event. A later auditor could inspect the current plan but could not determine the prior status or completion attribution from the event itself.
- **Correction:** the update route now snapshots the persisted plan after acquiring the Analysis lock and before mutation, then stores that snapshot as `before_state` alongside the resulting state.
- **Regression coverage:** the SQLite persistence test verifies both the transition from PLANNED to COMPLETED and reopening to IN_PROGRESS, including prior `completed_by` and cleared post-update attribution.

### 2026-10-27 bind report specimen identity to its analyzed VCF

- **Finding:** `create_analysis` persists both the input VCF artifact ID and its specimen ID, but report generation selected the newest specimen for the case. In a family/multi-specimen case this could label the report with a relative's specimen even though the analysis used the proband VCF. Legacy analyses without a pinned specimen were also silently assigned the newest row.
- **Correction:** report specimen resolution now follows the analysis's configured input VCF and specimen, verifies artifact type, case ownership, specimen ownership, analysis lineage, and agreement between the artifact and configured specimen. A legacy analysis can use a specimen only when exactly one exists in the case; ambiguous multi-specimen cases fail report eligibility with an explicit reason. Report content uses the same resolver.
- **Regression coverage:** tests verify that the VCF-bound specimen is used even when a different case specimen is newer, that conflicting artifact/configured specimen identity blocks report eligibility, and that ambiguous unpinned legacy analyses are rejected.
- **Verification:** Linux command `PYTHONPATH=. DATABASE_URL=sqlite:////tmp/siraloom-specimen-report-final.sqlite REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0 GENEBE_ENABLED=false GNOMAD_ENABLED=false FIREBASE_AUTH_REQUIRED=false .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp=/tmp/siraloom-specimen-report-final-tests --tb=short tests/test_report*.py tests/test_reporting*.py tests/integration/test_report_source_snapshot_postgres.py tests/integration/test_report_generation_analysis_lock_postgres.py` — **146 passed, 3 skipped**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. Skips require PostgreSQL (run through CI separately). Ruff F/I, Python compilation, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Status/limits:** **VERIFIED** for report-to-input specimen linkage, mismatch blocking, and ambiguity handling in the executed local suite. This verifies persisted identity linkage, not biological sample identity or a laboratory chain-of-custody process.

### 2026-10-27 include effective case phenotypes in reproducibility fingerprints

- **Finding:** the phenotype evidence worker and report builder consume both analysis-specific observations and case-level observations (`analysis_id IS NULL`), but `build_reproducibility_manifest` fingerprinted only analysis-specific phenotype rows. A case-level HPO update could therefore be absent from the scientific-output fingerprint even though it is an input to the workflow.
- **Correction:** the manifest now includes the case-level and analysis-specific phenotype observations consumed for the selected analysis, records each row's scope, and orders observations deterministically.
- **Regression coverage:** a manifest test adds a case-level HPO observation to an otherwise unchanged analysis and verifies that its scientific-output fingerprint changes.
- **Verification:** `PYTHONPATH=. DATABASE_URL=sqlite:////tmp/siraloom-case-phenotype-manifest.sqlite REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0 GENEBE_ENABLED=false GNOMAD_ENABLED=false FIREBASE_AUTH_REQUIRED=false .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider --basetemp=/tmp/siraloom-case-phenotype-manifest-tests --tb=short tests/test_reanalysis_reproducibility_manifest.py` — **6 passed**, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. (The complete manifest suite contains six tests.)
- **Status/limits:** **PARTIAL**. The fingerprint now reflects current effective case-level phenotype inputs, but the case-level observations are append-only current context rather than a frozen analysis-time snapshot. A separate durable capture of exactly which case inputs were consumed at stage execution remains necessary for historical replay guarantees.
